| To work with APIs that already give you a `CompletableFuture` | `CompletableFuture`, with `CompletableFuturePath` or its instance |
| Work that waits until you run it, on virtual threads, alone or concurrently | [VTask](vtask_monad.md) |
| A side effect that waits until you run it, on the calling thread | [IO](io_monad.md) |
| A value worked out once and kept, with no side effect | [Lazy](lazy_monad.md) |
| Typed errors inside an asynchronous result | The [EitherT](../transformers/eithert_transformer.md) transformer |

---

**Previous:** [Lazy](lazy_monad.md)
**Next:** [VTask](vtask_monad.md)

<!-- Source: monads/vtask_monad.md -->

<!-- description: Run blocking Java work on virtual threads with VTask: compose it on a VTaskPath, run lookups at the same time with Par, and keep typed errors on VResultPath. -->

# VTask: Deferred Tasks and Virtual Threads

_Describe work that may block, then run it when you choose, alone or alongside other work._

<!-- source: Rob Pike, "Concurrency is not Parallelism", a talk at Heroku's Waza conference, 11 January 2012; slide text checked against go.dev/talks/2012/waza.slide -->
> _"Concurrency is about dealing with lots of things at once. Parallelism is about doing lots of things at once."_
>
> — Rob Pike, *Concurrency is not Parallelism*

A `VTask` is built for the dealing. `Par` and `Scope` can keep thousands of tasks waiting on replies at once, each on a virtual thread, while only as many run at any moment as the machine has cores.

~~~admonish abstract title="VTask at a glance"
| | |
|---|---|
| **Models** | Work that may block or fail, described now and run when you choose, alone or at the same time as other work |
| **Replaces** | A `Callable` submitted to an `ExecutorService`, and the `Future` you wait on |
| **Effect Path** | [`VTaskPath`](../effect/path_vtask.md), and [`VResultPath`](../effect/path_vresult.md) for a typed error |
| **Instances** | `Functor`, `Applicative`, `Monad`, `MonadError<Throwable>` |
| **Choose it when** | The work throws a checked exception, or should run at the same time as other work; otherwise [IO](io_monad.md), or compare every type in [Choosing a Type](supported-types.md#choosing-a-type) |
~~~

~~~admonish info title="What You'll Learn"
- Describe work that blocks or throws a checked exception, and run it when you choose
- Run two lookups at the same time on a `VTaskPath`, and recover from one that fails
- Keep a refusal the caller expects as a typed `Left`, not an exception, on a `VResultPath`
- Say which thread runs a task, and what `run()`, `runSafe()` and `runAsync()` do with a failure
- Use `VTask` in generic code through its `MonadError` instance
~~~

~~~admonish example title="See Example Code"
**The code on this page is [VTaskBook.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/main/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/vtask/VTaskBook.java)**: the page includes it, so the build compiles and runs it. [VTaskBookTest.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/vtask/VTaskBookTest.java) holds what the page says each method does.
~~~

---

## Describing work that may block {#describing-work-that-may-block}

In plain Java, you run blocking work on another thread by submitting a `Callable` to an `ExecutorService`. With `Executors.newVirtualThreadPerTaskExecutor()`, each task gets a [virtual thread](https://openjdk.org/jeps/444) of its own, which costs little while it waits. The work starts as soon as you submit it, and `Future.get()` makes you catch `InterruptedException` and `ExecutionException`. A `VTask<A>` holds a `Callable` that nothing has run yet. You compose tasks first, and choose afterwards how to run them. `run()` runs a task on the calling thread, `runAsync()` on a virtual thread of its own, and `Par` at the same time as other tasks. A `Callable` may throw a checked exception, which the `Supplier` behind an [IO](io_monad.md) cannot.

The code on this page uses these imports, with `Order` from the order records the [Optics](../optics/ch_intro.md) chapter introduces:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import java.io.IOException;
import java.math.BigDecimal;
import org.higherkindedj.example.book.optics.cast.Order;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.effect.Path;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.effect.VTaskPath;
```

Pricing an order in euros needs two lookups, the exchange rate and a courier's quote, and neither service is called until you run the task. Each call blocks until its service answers, and may throw an `IOException`:

```java
  // Two remote services: each call blocks until it answers, and may throw an IOException
  interface Rates {
    BigDecimal toEuros() throws IOException;
  }

  interface Courier {
    BigDecimal quote(Order order) throws IOException;
  }

```

## On a `VTaskPath` {#on-a-vtaskpath}

Application code holds tasks on a `VTaskPath`. `Path.vtask` puts a `Callable` on one, `via` chains a step that needs the previous result, and `map` changes the value, as on every Path. `handleError` gives a value in place of a failure, here `CACHED_RATE`, a stored rate of 1.15 for when the rate service cannot answer. `parZipWith` runs two tasks at the same time and joins their results:

```java
  static VTaskPath<BigDecimal> priceInEuros(Order order, Rates rates, Courier courier) {
    VTaskPath<BigDecimal> rate = Path.vtask(rates::toEuros).handleError(error -> CACHED_RATE);
    VTaskPath<BigDecimal> shipping = Path.vtask(() -> courier.quote(order));

    // The two lookups run at the same time, each on a virtual thread
    return rate.parZipWith(shipping, (r, s) -> inEuros(order, r, s));
  }

```

~~~admonish warning title="A preview API"
`parZipWith` runs its tasks through `Par`, which with [`Scope`](vtask_scope.md) and `ScopeJoiner` is built on Java 25's structured concurrency API, [JEP 505](https://openjdk.org/jeps/505), a preview feature. Their class files are marked as using it. Code that names `Par`, `Scope` or `ScopeJoiner` needs `--enable-preview` to compile and to run. Code that reaches them only through a Path, such as a `VTaskPath`'s `zipWith`, `zipWith3`, `parZipWith` or `race`, compiles without it and needs it when it runs. The [HKJ Gradle plugin](../tooling/gradle_plugin.md) adds it by default. The JDK's API may change in a later release; your code calls the library's types, not the JDK's.
~~~

`LIVE_RATES` and `RATES_DOWN` stand in for the rate service, answering 1.17 or failing. `COURIER` stands in for the courier, which quotes 7.99 and counts its quotes in `QUOTES`. `ORDER` is worth 92.50 in pounds, and `inEuros` adds the shipping and converts the sum, so the live rate gives (92.50 + 7.99) × 1.17 = 117.57:

```java
    VTaskPath<BigDecimal> price = priceInEuros(ORDER, LIVE_RATES, COURIER);
    int quotesBeforeRunning = QUOTES.get();
    // 0
    BigDecimal euros = price.unsafeRun();
    // 117.57
    BigDecimal withCachedRate = priceInEuros(ORDER, RATES_DOWN, COURIER).unsafeRun();
    // 115.56
```

Building the path asks neither service. `unsafeRun()` waits on the calling thread while both lookups run. It is where the work happens, so call it once, at the edge of the application. Each run asks the services again. On a `VTaskPath`, `zipWith` runs both paths at once too, as `parZipWith` does.

A `VTaskPath` also cancels a slow task with `timeout(duration)`, and retries with `withRetry`, as [VTaskPath](../effect/path_vtask.md) shows. When the time runs out, the task is interrupted, and the `TimeoutException` arrives once it has stopped, so the task is not left running. A task that ignores interruption, or blocks in `CompletableFuture.join()`, which an interrupt cannot reach, holds the failure back until it finishes. To fail on time instead, see [Timeouts](../effect/path_vtask.md#timeouts).

## A refusal as a value, on a `VResultPath` {#on-a-vresultpath}

An exception suits a failure nobody plans for, such as a service that is down. A refusal the caller expects belongs in the result. A `VResultPath<E, A>` holds a `VTask<Either<E, A>>`, and works as an [EitherPath](../effect/path_either.md) whose steps may block: `via` stops at the first `Left`, and `recover` and `mapError` work on the error. The courier refuses to collect an order that has already shipped. These are the extra imports, with `OrderError`, the order service's refusals, from the [Either](either_monad.md#failure-as-a-value) page:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import org.higherkindedj.example.book.foundations.OrderError;
import org.higherkindedj.example.book.optics.cast.OrderStatus;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.effect.VResultPath;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.either.Either;
```

```java
  // The courier refuses a collection for an order that has already shipped
  static VResultPath<OrderError, String> bookCollection(Order order) {
    return Path.vresultDefer(
        () ->
            order.status() == OrderStatus.SHIPPED
                ? Either.left(OrderError.alreadyShipped(order.id()))
                : Either.right("collection booked for " + order.customer().name()));
  }

  static VResultPath<OrderError, String> confirmCollection(Order order) {
    return bookCollection(order)
        .via(booking -> Path.<OrderError, String>vresultRight(booking + ", confirmed"));
  }

```

`SHIPPED` is `ORDER` once it has shipped. `run()` gives the `VTask`, and running that gives the `Either`:

```java
    Either<OrderError, String> booked = confirmCollection(ORDER).run().run();
    // Right(collection booked for Ada, confirmed)
    Either<OrderError, String> refused = confirmCollection(SHIPPED).run().run();
    // Left(AlreadyShipped[orderId=00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001])
```

`withTimeout(duration, onTimeout)` gives a typed `Left` when the time runs out. Like a `VTaskPath`'s `timeout`, it interrupts the task and waits for it to stop first. An interrupted booking may already have reached the courier, so the `Left` says the outcome is unknown, not that nothing was booked. A `VResultPath`'s `zipWith` runs its paths one after another and stops at the first `Left`. To run typed work at the same time, see [VResultPath](../effect/path_vresult.md#outcome-aware-structured-concurrency).

## Running a task {#running-a-task}

`VTaskPath.run()` hands you the `VTask` inside, without running it. That `VTask` runs in one of three ways. Its `run()` runs it on the calling thread and waits. `runSafe()` does the same, and holds a failure in a [Try](try_monad.md); prefer it in code that handles failures as values. `runAsync()` starts it on a new virtual thread and returns a `CompletableFuture`. Running a task does not move it to a virtual thread: `main` runs on a platform thread, an ordinary thread the operating system schedules, and `run()` keeps the task there. Only `runAsync()`, `timeout`, `Par` and `Scope` move work to a virtual thread. These are the extra imports:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.trymonad.Try;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vtask.VTask;
```

```java
    VTask<Boolean> onAVirtualThread = VTask.of(() -> Thread.currentThread().isVirtual());

    boolean fromRun = onAVirtualThread.run();
    // false
    boolean fromRunAsync = onAVirtualThread.runAsync().join();
    // true
```

On a failure, a `VTask`'s `runSafe()` holds the exception the task threw, a checked one included:

```java
    VTask<BigDecimal> rateWhileDown = VTask.of(RATES_DOWN::toEuros);

    Try<BigDecimal> held = rateWhileDown.runSafe();
    // Failure(java.io.IOException: the rate service is down)
```

`run()` throws an unchecked exception as it is. It declares no checked exception, so it wraps a checked one in a `VTaskExecutionException`, whose `getCause()` gives the original. The future from `runAsync()` fails with the task's exception: `join()` throws it wrapped in a `CompletionException`, and `get()` in an `ExecutionException`.

## Running tasks at the same time {#parallel-composition-with-par}

`Par` runs tasks at the same time, each on a virtual thread of its own, and waits for them all. `map2` and `zip` join two tasks, and `all` a list of them. `traverse` runs the task a function gives for each item of a list. `race` keeps the first task to succeed and cancels the rest. `Par.all` is close to `ExecutorService.invokeAll`, and `race` to `invokeAny`. What `Par` adds is that when one task fails, `map2`, `zip`, `all` and `traverse` cancel the others and fail with its exception. `parZipWith` on a `VTaskPath` runs its two tasks through `Par.map2`. These are the extra imports:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import java.util.Collections;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vtask.Par;
```

`COURIERS` holds three couriers, which quote 7.99, 6.50 and 9.20. The three quotes take as long as the slowest of them, not the three added together:

```java
    VTask<BigDecimal> cheapest =
        Par.traverse(COURIERS, courier -> VTask.of(() -> courier.quote(ORDER)))
            .map(Collections::min);

    BigDecimal best = cheapest.run();
    // 6.50
```

## Creating instances {#creating-instances}

`VTask.of(callable)` describes work that may throw any exception, and `VTask.delay(supplier)` work that throws no checked exception. `VTask.exec(runnable)` describes work that gives [`Unit`](../hkts/core-concepts.md#unit). `VTask.succeed(value)` and `VTask.fail(exception)` give a task that has already succeeded or failed. `VTask.blocking(callable)` is `of` under a name that says the work blocks, and runs the same.

On a Path, `Path.vtask` matches `of`, `Path.vtaskExec` matches `exec`, `Path.vtaskPure` matches `succeed` and `Path.vtaskFail` matches `fail`, and `Path.vtaskPath(task)` takes a `VTask` you already have. For a `VResultPath`, they are `Path.vresultDefer`, `Path.vresultRight`, `Path.vresultLeft` and `Path.vresult(task)`.

### A task always gives a value {#a-task-always-gives-a-value}

A `VTask` that succeeds gives a value, never `null`. `VTask.succeed(null)` throws `NullPointerException` at the call. A callable, supplier, `map` function or recovery function that returns null fails the task when it runs, and the exception names that function, as in `callable must not return null`. A task with nothing to return gives `Unit.INSTANCE`, as `VTask.exec` does, and one whose value may be missing says so with a `Maybe`. These are the extra imports:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.Unit;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.maybe.Maybe;
```

```java
  // Nothing to return: give Unit.INSTANCE, as VTask.exec does
  static final VTask<Unit> LOGGED = VTask.exec(() -> System.out.println("priced " + ORDER.id()));

  // A value that may be missing: say so with a Maybe
  static final VTask<Maybe<String>> NICKNAME =
      VTask.of(() -> Maybe.fromNullable(System.getenv("NICKNAME")));

```

So `VTask.of(() -> { work(); return null; })`, typed `VTask<Void>`, fails when it runs. Use `VTask.exec`, or return `Unit.INSTANCE`. NullAway flags that lambda only with the options in [What a nullness checker sees](../hkts/core-concepts.md#what-a-nullness-checker-sees). `VTask` is itself a functional interface, so you can write a task as a lambda, which NullAway checks with no options set; `run()`, `runSafe()` and `runAsync()` refuse a null from one of those too.

The factories and functions of every lazy type follow the same rule: `IO`, `VStream`, `State`, `Reader`, `Writer`, `Lazy`, `Trampoline`, `Free` and `Context`, and every Effect Path over one. A stream refuses a null element. A nullness checker flags a hand-written `IO` or `Reader` lambda that returns null, and an `IOPath` refuses one when it runs. Only [`Id`](identity.md) holds a null, since every optic's `modify` runs through it. A `CompletableFuture` outside a Path keeps the JDK's null too.

## Methods {#methods}

| Method | What it does |
|---|---|
| `VTask.of(callable)`, `delay(supplier)`, `exec(runnable)` | Describe work; nothing runs until the task does |
| `VTask.succeed(value)`, `VTask.fail(exception)` | Give a task that has already succeeded or failed |
| `map(f)`, `flatMap(f)`, `via(f)`, `then(next)` | Build a new task, running nothing |
| `recover(f)`, `recoverWith(f)`, `mapError(f)` | Give a value or another task in place of a failure, or change the exception |
| `timeout(duration)` | Runs the task on a virtual thread of its own; if it has not finished in time, interrupts it and fails with a `TimeoutException` once it has stopped |
| `run()`, `runSafe()` | Run the task on the calling thread, throwing its failure or holding it in a `Try` |
| `runAsync()` | Starts the task on a new virtual thread, and returns a `CompletableFuture` |
| `Par.map2`, `zip`, `all`, `traverse`, `race` | Run tasks at the same time, each on a virtual thread |
| `Path.vtask(callable)`, `Path.vresultDefer(supplier)` | Put work on a `VTaskPath`, or on a `VResultPath` |
| `VTASK.widen(task)`, `VTASK.narrow(kind)`, `VTASK.run(kind)` | Move a task in and out of `Kind`, or run one |

## Working with Kind {#working-with-kind}

Generic code, written once for any container, takes `VTask` through its `MonadError` instance, a `VTaskMonad`, from `Instances.monadError(vtask())`. Its error type is `Throwable`, as [MonadError](../functional/monad_error.md) shows. The instance works over `Kind<VTaskKind.Witness, A>`, so you widen a task to pass it in, and run the result with `VTASK.run`. These are the extra imports:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import static org.higherkindedj.hkt.instances.Witnesses.vtask;
import static org.higherkindedj.hkt.vtask.VTaskKindHelper.VTASK;

import org.higherkindedj.hkt.Kind;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.MonadError;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.instances.Instances;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vtask.VTaskKind;
```

```java
    MonadError<VTaskKind.Witness, Throwable> tasks = Instances.monadError(vtask());

    Kind<VTaskKind.Witness, BigDecimal> rate =
        tasks.handleErrorWith(
            VTASK.widen(VTask.of(RATES_DOWN::toEuros)), error -> tasks.of(CACHED_RATE));
    BigDecimal fallback = VTASK.run(rate);
    // 1.15
```

The handler receives the exception the task threw. This one ignores it and gives a constant, which the instance's [`recover`](../functional/monad_error.md#constant-fallbacks-recover-and-recoverwith) says directly, given the widened task and `CACHED_RATE`. Those instance-level forms take the `Kind` first. The fluent `recover` on a `VTask` takes a function of the exception.

The instance's `ap`, and `map2` to `map5`, run their tasks one after another, so a failure in the first stops the second from running; `Par` runs tasks at the same time. Widening allocates nothing: `VTask` implements `VTaskKind` itself, so `widen` checks for `null` and returns the same object.

## When to use `VTask` {#when-to-use-vtask}

| You need | Use |
|---|---|
| Work that may block or throw a checked exception, run when you choose | `VTask`, on a `VTaskPath` in application code |
| Two or more tasks at the same time, each on a virtual thread | `parZipWith`, or `Par` |
| A refusal the caller expects, as a typed `Left` rather than an exception | `VResultPath` |
| Tasks that start and end together, joined the way you choose | [Structured Concurrency](vtask_scope.md) |
| A resource released however the work ends | [Resource Management](vtask_resource.md) |
| A side effect with no checked exception, never run alongside other work | [IO](io_monad.md) |
| Work already started, from an API that gives you a future | [CompletableFuture](cf_monad.md) |
| Many values, produced one at a time | [VStream](vstream.md) |

~~~admonish info title="Hands-On Learning"
Practise `VTask` in [TutorialVTask](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/tutorial/concurrency/TutorialVTask.java) (9 exercises), and `VTaskPath` in [TutorialVTaskPath](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/tutorial/concurrency/TutorialVTaskPath.java) (9 exercises), both part of the [VTask journey](../tutorials/concurrency/vtask_journey.md).
~~~

**Benchmarks:** `VTaskBenchmark` and `VTaskPathBenchmark` measure building and running tasks, and `VTaskParBenchmark` the `Par` combinators. `VTaskVsIOBenchmark` and `VTaskVsPlatformThreadsBenchmark` compare `VTask` with `IO` and with a pool of platform threads. [Benchmarks & Performance](../benchmarks.md) says how to run them.

---

**Previous:** [CompletableFuture](cf_monad.md)
**Next:** [Structured Concurrency](vtask_scope.md)

<!-- Source: monads/vtask_scope.md -->

<!-- description: Structured concurrency in Java with Scope: fork VTasks, join them all, the first success or every error, cancel the rest, and time the whole group out. -->

# Scope: Structured Concurrency

_Start tasks together, end them together, and join their results the way you choose._

<!-- source: Nathaniel J. Smith, "Notes on structured concurrency, or: Go statement considered harmful", vorpus.org, 25 April 2018; text checked against the post -->
> _"Here's the core idea: every time our control splits into multiple concurrent paths, we want to make sure that they join up again."_
>
> — Nathaniel J. Smith, *Notes on structured concurrency, or: Go statement considered harmful*

A `Scope` is where they join up: it forks its tasks, and the `VTask` from its `join()` ends only when every task has finished or been cancelled.

~~~admonish info title="What You'll Learn"
- Fork tasks into a `Scope` and join them, so none outlives the scope that started it
- Choose how they are joined: every result, the first success, the first to finish, or every failure
- Hold a scope's failure as a value, and time the whole group out, cancelling the tasks still running
- Run one `Scope` again, retry it, or share it between threads
- Use a `ScopeJoiner` with a `Scope`, or with a `StructuredTaskScope` you open yourself
~~~

~~~admonish example title="See Example Code"
**The code on this page is [ScopeBook.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/main/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/scope/ScopeBook.java)**: the page includes it, so the build compiles and runs it. [ScopeBookTest.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/scope/ScopeBookTest.java) holds what the page says each method does. [ScopeExample.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/main/java/org/higherkindedj/example/effect/ScopeExample.java) is a longer example.
~~~

---

## Tasks that start and end together {#tasks-that-start-and-end-together}

A task handed to an `ExecutorService` can outlive the method that submitted it, and a failure in one task leaves the others running. Java 25's `StructuredTaskScope` ([JEP 505](https://openjdk.org/jeps/505)) ties tasks to a block instead, as [Nathaniel J. Smith's essay](https://vorpus.org/blog/notes-on-structured-concurrency-or-go-statement-considered-harmful/) argued for: they are forked inside a `try`, and the block does not end until each has finished. A `Scope` holds the same shape as a value. You fork `VTask`s into it, and `join()` gives a `VTask` that, when it runs, opens a `StructuredTaskScope`, forks each task on a virtual thread of its own, and waits for them.

~~~admonish warning title="A preview API"
`Scope` and `ScopeJoiner` are built on `StructuredTaskScope`, a preview feature in Java 25, so code that uses them needs `--enable-preview` to compile and to run, as the [VTask](vtask_monad.md#on-a-vtaskpath) page explains.
~~~

The code on this page uses these imports, with `Order` and `LineItem` from the order records the [Optics](../optics/ch_intro.md) chapter introduces:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import java.io.IOException;
import java.math.BigDecimal;
import java.util.List;
import org.higherkindedj.example.book.optics.cast.LineItem;
import org.higherkindedj.example.book.optics.cast.Order;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.validated.Validated;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vtask.Scope;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vtask.VTask;
```

Booking delivery for an order means asking couriers for a quote, and checking that the warehouse has the stock. Each call blocks until its service answers:

```java
  // A courier quotes for an order, and a warehouse counts its stock of a product. Each call
  // blocks until its service answers, and may throw an IOException.
  interface Courier {
    BigDecimal quote(Order order) throws IOException;
  }

  interface Warehouse {
    int inStock(String sku) throws IOException;
  }

```

`Scope.allSucceed()` waits for every task and gives their results in the order they were forked. `Scope.anySucceed()` gives the first success, and `Scope.firstComplete()` the first task to finish, success or failure; both cancel the tasks still running. `forkAll` forks a list of tasks, and `fork` one:

```java
  static VTask<BigDecimal> quoteFrom(Courier courier, Order order) {
    return VTask.of(() -> courier.quote(order));
  }

  static List<VTask<BigDecimal>> quotesFrom(List<Courier> couriers, Order order) {
    return couriers.stream().map(courier -> quoteFrom(courier, order)).toList();
  }

  // Every courier's quote, in the order they were forked
  static VTask<List<BigDecimal>> allQuotes(List<Courier> couriers, Order order) {
    return Scope.<BigDecimal>allSucceed().forkAll(quotesFrom(couriers, order)).join();
  }

  // The first courier to answer with a quote
  static VTask<BigDecimal> firstQuote(List<Courier> couriers, Order order) {
    return Scope.<BigDecimal>anySucceed().forkAll(quotesFrom(couriers, order)).join();
  }

  // Whichever courier answers first, with a quote or a failure
  static VTask<BigDecimal> firstAnswer(List<Courier> couriers, Order order) {
    return Scope.<BigDecimal>firstComplete().forkAll(quotesFrom(couriers, order)).join();
  }

```

`ORDER` is an order for two lamps and a shade. `COURIERS` holds three couriers, which quote 7.99, 6.50 and 9.20, and `DOWN` stands in for a courier whose service is down:

```java
    List<BigDecimal> quotes = allQuotes(COURIERS, ORDER).run();
    // [7.99, 6.50, 9.20]
    BigDecimal answered = firstQuote(List.of(DOWN, COURIERS.get(1), DOWN), ORDER).run();
    // 6.50
```

Building a scope runs nothing, and each run forks its tasks afresh.

## Choosing a joiner {#choosing-a-joiner}

Java's `StructuredTaskScope` takes a `Joiner`, which decides when the scope is done and what its `join()` returns. A `Scope`'s factory chooses one:

| Factory | Gives | When a task fails | Use it for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `allSucceed()` | Every result, in fork order | Cancels the rest, and fails with its exception | Lookups that must all succeed |
| `anySucceed()` | The first success, cancelling the rest | Fails only if every task fails, with one of their exceptions | Redundant requests, any one of which will do |
| `firstComplete()` | The first task to finish, success or failure, cancelling the rest | Fails if that first task failed | Taking whichever answer comes first |
| `accumulating(mapper)` | A `Validated`: every result, or every failure | Keeps it, mapped by `mapper`, and waits for the rest | Checks that should all report |
| `withJoiner(joiner)` | What the `ScopeJoiner` decides | As the joiner decides | One joining policy shared by several scopes |

For a fallback that answers when the fast path fails, use `anySucceed`: `firstComplete` takes a failure if it comes first.

The scope is opened on the thread that runs the `VTask`, so its tasks see that thread's `ScopedValue` bindings, such as the [request context](../effect/context_request.md). `named(...)` gives the scope a name, which a thread dump in JSON format shows.

## Keeping every failure {#keeping-every-failure}

A form, or an order, that fails on its first problem sends the user away to fix it, and back to find the next. `Scope.accumulating(mapper)` runs every task to the end, cancelling none, and gives a [Validated](validated_monad.md): every result if all succeeded, or every failure, each mapped by `mapper`, in fork order. Checking each line of the order against the warehouse:

```java
  static VTask<LineItem> checkStock(Warehouse warehouse, LineItem line) {
    return VTask.of(
        () -> {
          int inStock = warehouse.inStock(line.sku());
          if (inStock < line.quantity()) {
            throw new IllegalStateException(
                line.sku() + ": " + line.quantity() + " ordered, " + inStock + " in stock");
          }
          return line;
        });
  }

  // Every line is checked, and every shortfall kept
  static VTask<Validated<List<String>, List<LineItem>>> checkLines(
      Warehouse warehouse, Order order) {
    return Scope.<String, LineItem>accumulating(Throwable::getMessage)
        .forkAll(order.lines().stream().map(line -> checkStock(warehouse, line)).toList())
        .join();
  }

```

`SHORT` stands in for a warehouse with one lamp and no shades, and `WELL_STOCKED` for one with plenty of both:

```java
    Validated<List<String>, List<LineItem>> shortfalls = checkLines(SHORT, ORDER).run();
    // Invalid([LAMP: 2 ordered, 1 in stock, SHADE: 1 ordered, 0 in stock])
    boolean allInStock = checkLines(WELL_STOCKED, ORDER).run().isValid();
    // true
```

Both shortfalls are reported at once. Each scope calls `mapper` on the thread that joins it, so a `mapper` shared by scopes running at once must be thread-safe.

## Holding the result as a value {#holding-the-result-as-a-value}

`join()` gives a task that fails when the scope does. `joinSafe()`, `joinEither()` and `joinMaybe()` give one that always succeeds, holding the failure as a `Try`, an `Either` with the exception on the left, or a `Maybe` that is `Nothing`. Use them to handle a failure inside the pipeline, rather than where the task is run. These are the extra imports:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.either.Either;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.trymonad.Try;
```

```java
    Either<Throwable, List<BigDecimal>> refused =
        Scope.<BigDecimal>allSucceed().fork(quoteFrom(DOWN, ORDER)).joinEither().run();
    // Left(java.io.IOException: the courier is down)
```

## Timing a scope out {#timing-a-scope-out}

`timeout(duration)` bounds the whole scope. When it expires, the tasks still running are interrupted, those not yet started never start, and the run fails with a `TimeoutException` once they have stopped. `SILENT` stands in for a courier that never answers. This is the extra import:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import java.time.Duration;
```

```java
    Try<List<BigDecimal>> late =
        Scope.<BigDecimal>allSucceed()
            .timeout(Duration.ofMillis(50))
            .fork(quoteFrom(SILENT, ORDER))
            .joinSafe()
            .run();
    // Failure(java.util.concurrent.TimeoutException: Scope timed out after PT0.05S)
```

A scope's timeout cancels the tasks still running, as [`VTask.timeout`](vtask_monad.md#on-a-vtaskpath) cancels its one task. A task that ignores interruption, such as one spinning in a loop, holds the failure back until it finishes. Each run's timeout starts when that run opens its scope. A timeout of zero or less expires at once, so the run fails unless its tasks have already finished.

## Running a scope again {#running-a-scope-again}

A `Scope` is a description, like the `VTask` its `join()` returns. Each run of that `VTask` opens a `StructuredTaskScope` of its own, with a new `Joiner`. So you can run it again, retry it, or run it from several threads at once, and each run joins only the tasks it forked.

Here the quote service is busy on its first call, and [`Retry`](../resilience/retry.md#vtask-native-retry) runs the same `prices` task again. These are the extra imports:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicInteger;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.resilience.Retry;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.resilience.RetryPolicy;
```

```java
    AtomicInteger quoteCalls = new AtomicInteger();
    VTask<String> quote =
        VTask.of(
            () -> {
              if (quoteCalls.incrementAndGet() == 1) {
                throw new IllegalStateException("quote service busy");
              }
              return "quote";
            });
    VTask<List<String>> prices =
        Scope.<String>allSucceed().fork(quote).fork(VTask.succeed("rates")).join();

    List<String> retried =
        Retry.retryTask(prices, RetryPolicy.fixed(3, Duration.ofMillis(100))).run();
    // [quote, rates]
    List<String> runAgain = prices.run();
    // [quote, rates]
```

The first attempt fails. The second forks both tasks again and joins only those, so the first attempt's failure cannot fail it, and it returns `[quote, rates]`. Running `prices` once more gives the same list. A retry runs every task again, `rates` included, so retry a `Scope` whose tasks are safe to repeat.

## A joiner of your own {#a-joiner-of-your-own}

A `ScopeJoiner` is a joining policy on its own: `ScopeJoiner.allSucceed()`, `anySucceed()`, `firstComplete()` and `accumulating(mapper)` match the `Scope` factories. `Scope.withJoiner` takes one, and `ScopeJoiner.joiner()` builds the JDK's `Joiner` for a scope you open yourself. These are the extra imports:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import java.util.concurrent.StructuredTaskScope;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vtask.ScopeJoiner;
```

`twoQuotesByHand` opens a scope with a `Joiner` from the policy, as Java's own API asks:

```java
  static List<BigDecimal> twoQuotesByHand(
      ScopeJoiner<BigDecimal, List<BigDecimal>> policy, Order order) throws InterruptedException {
    try (var scope = StructuredTaskScope.open(policy.joiner())) {
      scope.fork(() -> COURIERS.get(0).quote(order));
      scope.fork(() -> COURIERS.get(1).quote(order));
      return scope.join();
    }
  }

```

The same policy serves a `Scope` and the scope opened by hand:

```java
    ScopeJoiner<BigDecimal, List<BigDecimal>> everyQuote = ScopeJoiner.allSucceed();

    // The same policy for a Scope, and for the StructuredTaskScope opened by hand
    List<BigDecimal> viaScope =
        Scope.withJoiner(everyQuote).forkAll(quotesFrom(COURIERS, ORDER)).join().run();
    // [7.99, 6.50, 9.20]
    List<BigDecimal> byHand = twoQuotesByHand(everyQuote, ORDER);
    // [7.99, 6.50]
```

A `ScopeJoiner` holds its configuration, not a scope's state. Each call to `joiner()` returns a new `Joiner`, because a `StructuredTaskScope` needs one that no other scope has used, so one `ScopeJoiner` serves any number of scopes.

`ScopeJoiner.firstSuccessEither()` races tasks that each give an `Either`. The first `Right` wins and cancels the rest. A `Left` does not end the race; if every task gives one, the join gives a `Left` of all their errors, in fork order. A task that throws is not collected: with no `Right`, the join fails with its exception. [`VResultPath.firstSuccess`](../effect/path_vresult.md#outcome-aware-structured-concurrency) races typed paths through it.

`ScopeJoiner.resultEither()` is deprecated, and scheduled for removal in 0.6.0. Use `Scope.joinEither()` instead, or, with a scope you open yourself, the value its `join()` returns, or the cause of the `FailedException` it throws.

## Methods {#methods}

| Method | What it does |
|---|---|
| `Scope.allSucceed()`, `anySucceed()`, `firstComplete()`, `accumulating(mapper)`, `withJoiner(joiner)` | Build a scope that joins its tasks as [Choosing a joiner](#choosing-a-joiner) says |
| `fork(task)`, `forkAll(tasks)` | Give a new scope with the tasks added, running nothing |
| `timeout(duration)`, `named(name)` | Give a new scope that times out, or carries a name for thread dumps |
| `join()` | Gives a `VTask` that runs the scope and gives the joined result, or fails |
| `joinSafe()`, `joinEither()`, `joinMaybe()` | Give a `VTask` that holds a failure as a `Try`, an `Either` or a `Maybe` |
| `taskCount()`, `hasTimeout()`, `getTimeout()` | Say how many tasks are forked, and whether a timeout is set |
| `ScopeJoiner.allSucceed()` and the others, `firstSuccessEither()` | Give a joining policy, for `withJoiner` or a scope you open yourself |
| `joiner.joiner()` | Gives a new JDK `StructuredTaskScope.Joiner` |

## When to use a `Scope` {#when-to-use-a-scope}

| You need | Use |
|---|---|
| Tasks that must all succeed, joined into one value | [`Par`](vtask_monad.md#parallel-composition-with-par), or `parZipWith` on a `VTaskPath` |
| Tasks joined by first success, first to finish or every failure, or under one timeout | `Scope` |
| Typed results raced or gathered on a railway | [VResultPath](../effect/path_vresult.md#outcome-aware-structured-concurrency) |
| A resource held while the scope's tasks use it | [`Resource` with a `Scope`](vtask_resource.md#with-a-scope) |

~~~admonish info title="Hands-On Learning"
Practise `Scope` in [TutorialScope](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/tutorial/concurrency/TutorialScope.java) (10 exercises), part of the [Scope & Resource journey](../tutorials/concurrency/scope_resource_journey.md).
~~~

---

**Previous:** [VTask](vtask_monad.md)
**Next:** [Resource Management](vtask_resource.md)

<!-- Source: monads/vtask_resource.md -->

<!-- description: Bracket pattern for Java VTasks with Resource: acquire, use and release a connection however the task ends, like try-with-resources, and roll back on failure. -->

# Resource: Acquire, Use and Release

_Acquire something, use it, and release it however the work ends._

<!-- source: Seamus Heaney, "Scaffolding", in Death of a Naturalist (Faber and Faber, 1966); line checked against the text on poets.org -->
> _"And yet all this comes down when the job's done"_
>
> — Seamus Heaney, *Scaffolding*

A `Resource` is that scaffolding: put up before the work, and taken down after it, even when the job fails.

~~~admonish info title="What You'll Learn"
- Describe how to acquire and release something, and use it in a `VTask` that releases it when the task is done
- Say what is reported when the use fails, the release fails, or both do
- Compose resources that release in reverse order, and use one `Resource` many times
- Add finalisers, and an action that runs only when the use fails, such as a rollback
- Hold a resource while a `Scope`'s tasks use it
~~~

~~~admonish example title="See Example Code"
**The code on this page is [ResourceBook.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/main/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/resource/ResourceBook.java)**: the page includes it, so the build compiles and runs it. [ResourceBookTest.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/resource/ResourceBookTest.java) holds what the page says each method does.
~~~

---

## Acquire, use, release {#acquire-use-release}

Java's try-with-resources closes a resource when its block ends. A [`VTask`](vtask_monad.md) describes work that runs later, so a `try` around building one closes the resource before the work has started. A `Resource<A>` describes how to acquire an `A` and how to release it. `use` gives a `VTask` that, each time it runs, acquires, runs the task you give it, and releases what it acquired, whether that task succeeded, failed or was cancelled. This is the acquire-use-release, or bracket, pattern.

The code on this page uses these imports, with `Order` from the order records the [Optics](../optics/ch_intro.md) chapter introduces:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.concurrent.CopyOnWriteArrayList;
import java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicInteger;
import org.higherkindedj.example.book.optics.cast.Order;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vtask.Resource;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vtask.VTask;
```

Booking a courier happens on a session, which is opened first and must be closed after. `Session` stands in for one, and logs what happens to it:

```java
  // A booking session with the courier, which logs what happens to it
  record Session(String id, List<String> log) implements AutoCloseable {
    String book(Order order) {
      log.add("book on " + id);
      return "booked on " + id;
    }

    @Override
    public void close() {
      log.add("close " + id);
    }
  }

  // Each use opens a session of its own
  static Resource<Session> sessions(List<String> log) {
    AtomicInteger opened = new AtomicInteger();
    return Resource.fromAutoCloseable(
        () -> {
          Session session = new Session("session-" + opened.incrementAndGet(), log);
          log.add("open " + session.id());
          return session;
        });
  }

```

`ORDER` is a paid order:

```java
    List<String> log = new CopyOnWriteArrayList<>();
    Resource<Session> session = sessions(log);
    VTask<String> booking = session.use(s -> VTask.of(() -> s.book(ORDER)));

    int openedBeforeRunning = log.size();
    // 0
    booking.run();
    List<String> whatRan = List.copyOf(log);
    // [open session-1, book on session-1, close session-1]
```

Building the task opens nothing. Each run opens a session, books on it, and closes it, so running `booking` again opens `session-2`. `useSync(f)` does the same with a plain function rather than one that gives a task.

```mermaid
stateDiagram-v2
    accTitle: What happens to a resource during one use
    accDescr: A use acquires the resource. If the acquire fails, nothing is held, and nothing is released. Otherwise the resource is held while its task runs. If the task succeeds, the release runs. If the task fails or is cancelled, the onFailure actions run first, then the release. The finalisers run after the release, and the use ends with the task's result or its failure.
    state "the acquire failed" as NotHeld
    state "held" as Held
    state "the task succeeded" as Succeeded
    state "the task failed or was cancelled" as Failed
    state "the onFailure actions ran" as RolledBack
    state "released" as Released
    [*] --> NotHeld
    NotHeld --> [*] : nothing to release
    [*] --> Held : acquire
    Held --> Succeeded
    Held --> Failed
    Failed --> RolledBack
    Succeeded --> Released : release
    RolledBack --> Released : release
    Released --> [*] : finalisers
    classDef domain fill:#a6d189,stroke:#40a02b,color:#232634
    classDef step fill:#8caaee,stroke:#1e66f5,color:#232634
    classDef error fill:#e78284,stroke:#d20f39,color:#232634
    class Held domain
    class Succeeded step
    class Released step
    class NotHeld error
    class Failed error
    class RolledBack error
```

The diagram shows that every way out of a held resource passes through its release, after the [`onFailure` actions](#onfailure-callback) when the task fails, and before the [finalisers](#finalizer-behaviour). If the acquire fails, nothing is held, so neither the release, the `onFailure` actions nor the finalisers run.

### Creating a resource {#factory-methods}

| Factory | What it describes | For |
|---|---|---|
| `Resource.fromAutoCloseable(acquire)` | An `AutoCloseable`, closed after each use | Connections, sessions, streams, channels |
| `Resource.make(acquire, release)` | An acquire and a release of your own | Locks, handles, anything without `close()` |
| `Resource.pure(value)` | A value with nothing to release | Configuration, or a constant in a composition |

The acquire is a `Callable`, so it may throw a checked exception. A `Resource` never holds null: `pure(null)` throws at the call, and an acquire or a `map` function that returns null fails the use with a `NullPointerException`. Hold a value that may be absent as a `Maybe`, and acquire it with `make`.

## When the use fails {#exception-safety}

The session is closed whatever happens to the booking. These are the extra imports:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import java.io.IOException;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.trymonad.Try;
```

```java
    log.clear();
    Try<String> refused =
        session.use(s -> VTask.<String>fail(new IOException("the courier refused"))).runSafe();
    // Failure(java.io.IOException: the courier refused)
    List<String> closedAnyway = List.copyOf(log);
    // [open session-2, close session-2]
```

When the release throws as well, the use's failure is still the one reported, and the release's exception is suppressed onto it, as try-with-resources does. After a successful use, a release that throws fails the use with its own exception, so a writer whose final flush fails does not report success.

For `fromAutoCloseable`, the release is `close()`, which may throw a checked exception. The task that `use` returns reports it as it reports any checked failure: `run()` throws it wrapped in a `VTaskExecutionException`, and `runSafe()` holds it unwrapped.

A cancelled use is a failed one, so its resource is released too. The release runs with the thread's interrupt status cleared, so a cancellation does not cut a blocking release short, and the status is restored afterwards.

## Composing resources {#composing-resources}

`and` holds two resources at once, acquiring them in order and releasing them in reverse. `flatMap` acquires a second resource from the first, as try-with-resources nests a statement inside its connection, and releases the second first. Releasing in reverse means a resource that depends on another is released before the one it depends on. If a later acquire fails, the ones already held are released before the failure is reported. These are the extra imports:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vtask.Par;
```

```java
    log.clear();
    Resource<Par.Tuple2<Session, Session>> twoSessions = session.and(session);

    twoSessions.useSync(both -> both.first().book(ORDER) + ", " + both.second().book(ORDER)).run();
    List<String> inReverse = List.copyOf(log);
    // [open session-3, open session-4, book on session-3, book on session-4, close session-4,
    //   close session-3]

    // A shipping label printed on a session, voided after use
    log.clear();
    Resource<String> label =
        session.flatMap(s -> Resource.make(() -> s.id() + "-label", l -> log.add("void " + l)));

    label.useSync(String::length).run();
    List<String> labelFirst = List.copyOf(log);
    // [open session-5, void session-5-label, close session-5]
```

`and` gives a `Par.Tuple2`, a preview class, so code that calls `and` needs `--enable-preview`, whether or not it names `Par`, as the [VTask](vtask_monad.md#on-a-vtaskpath) page explains. `and(second, third)` holds three at once, as a `Par.Tuple3`. A `Resource` composed with `flatMap` or `and` reports a failed release as a `RuntimeException`, whose cause is the exception from the outermost release that threw.

`map(f)` changes the value a resource gives, and its release still receives the value the acquire returned. `map` takes a plain `Function`, so a checked exception has to be handled inside `f`.

## Using one resource many times {#using-one-resource-many-times}

A `Resource` holds nothing itself. Each `use` acquires its own resource and releases exactly that one. So build a `Resource` once and use it wherever it is needed: once per request, on several threads at once, or one use nested inside another. This holds however the `Resource` was composed:

```java
    log.clear();
    Resource<String> sessionIds = session.map(Session::id);

    List<String> both =
        sessionIds
            .use(outer -> sessionIds.use(inner -> VTask.succeed(List.of(outer, inner))))
            .run();
    // [session-6, session-7]
    List<String> innermostFirst = List.copyOf(log);
    // [open session-6, open session-7, close session-7, close session-6]
```

The nested use opens a second session, and each use closes the one it opened, innermost first. The use sees each session's id, and the release still closes the `Session` itself.

## Finalisers {#finalizer-behaviour}

`withFinalizer(action)` adds an action that runs after the release:

```java
    List<String> steps = new ArrayList<>();
    Resource<String> handle =
        Resource.make(() -> "handle", _ -> steps.add("release"))
            .withFinalizer(() -> steps.add("log the release"))
            .withFinalizer(() -> steps.add("record metrics"));

    handle.useSync(String::length).run();
    List<String> ranInOrder = List.copyOf(steps);
    // [release, log the release, record metrics]
```

Finalisers run in the order they were added, and each runs even when the release, or an earlier finaliser, throws. When the release and a finaliser both throw, the release's exception is reported, with the finaliser's suppressed onto it.

## Rolling back on failure {#onfailure-callback}

`onFailure(action)` adds an action that runs when the use fails, before the release. It receives the acquired resource, so it can undo partial work, such as rolling back a transaction. When the use succeeds, only the release runs:

```java
    List<String> ledger = new ArrayList<>();
    AtomicInteger begun = new AtomicInteger();
    Resource<String> transaction =
        Resource.make(() -> "tx-" + begun.incrementAndGet(), tx -> ledger.add("close " + tx))
            .onFailure(tx -> ledger.add("rollback " + tx));

    transaction.useSync(String::length).run();
    transaction.use(_ -> VTask.fail(new IllegalStateException("insert failed"))).runSafe();
    List<String> whatRanEachTime = List.copyOf(ledger);
    // [close tx-1, rollback tx-2, close tx-2]
```

The first use succeeds, so it only closes `tx-1`. The second fails, so `tx-2` is rolled back and then closed. The use fails when the function given to `use` throws, or the task it returns fails. A cancelled task fails too. A step composed after `onFailure` counts as well, when it fails while the resource is held:

- **`map`'s or `flatMap`'s function throws.**
- **The next acquire in `flatMap` or `and` fails.**

The action runs before its `Resource`'s release and before the finalisers added to it. Actions added one after another run most recently added first. If an action throws, the release still runs, and the use's failure is still the one reported.

## With a `Scope` {#with-a-scope}

A resource can be held while a [`Scope`](vtask_scope.md)'s tasks use it, and is released once the scope has joined them all. This is the extra import:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vtask.Scope;
```

```java
    log.clear();
    VTask<List<String>> bookTwice =
        session.use(
            s ->
                Scope.<String>allSucceed()
                    .fork(VTask.of(() -> s.book(ORDER)))
                    .fork(VTask.of(() -> s.book(ORDER)))
                    .join());

    List<String> bookings = bookTwice.run();
    // [booked on session-8, booked on session-8]
    List<String> closedAfterBoth = List.copyOf(log);
    // [open session-8, book on session-8, book on session-8, close session-8]
```

A resource the tasks share must be safe to use from several threads at once, as the session's log is here. Add an `onFailure` rollback, as in [Rolling back on failure](#onfailure-callback), and this shape runs a transaction's steps at the same time. When one step fails, the scope cancels the others, the rollback runs, then the release.

## Methods {#methods}

| Method | What it does |
|---|---|
| `Resource.fromAutoCloseable(acquire)`, `make(acquire, release)`, `pure(value)` | Describe a resource, acquiring nothing |
| `use(f)` | Gives a `VTask` that acquires, runs the task `f` gives for the resource, and releases |
| `useSync(f)` | The same, where `f` gives a plain value rather than a task |
| `map(f)` | Changes the value the resource gives |
| `flatMap(f)`, `and(other)`, `and(second, third)` | Hold resources together, releasing them in reverse order |
| `withFinalizer(action)` | Runs `action` after the release |
| `onFailure(action)` | Runs `action` on the resource when the use fails, before the release |

## When to use `Resource` {#when-to-use-resource}

| You need | Use |
|---|---|
| Something acquired and released around a `VTask`, however it ends | `Resource` |
| A resource used within one block of ordinary code | Java's try-with-resources |
| Cleanup after a task, with nothing to acquire | [`guarantee`](../effect/capabilities.md#effectful-io) on a `VTaskPath` |
| The same around an `IO` | [`bracket` or `withResource`](../effect/path_io.md) on an `IOPath` |
| A release that sees whether a typed result succeeded | [`bracketOutcome`](../effect/path_vresult.md) on a `VResultPath` |

~~~admonish info title="Hands-On Learning"
Practise `Resource` in [TutorialResource](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/tutorial/concurrency/TutorialResource.java) (10 exercises), part of the [Scope & Resource journey](../tutorials/concurrency/scope_resource_journey.md).
~~~

<!-- description: Lazy pull-based streams in Java with VStream: read a paged API one page at a time, transform elements with tasks, recover a failed element. -->

# VStream: Lazy Pull-Based Streams

_A sequence of elements, each produced by a task only when you ask for it._

<!-- source: Dan Powell, the archivist in Archive 81, the podcast by Marc Sollinger and Daniel Powell (Dead Signals, 2016); the wording has not been checked against a transcript -->
> _"Tape after tape. You don't get the whole story at once. You pull the next one, press play, and hope you're ready for what comes out."_
>
> — Dan Powell, *Archive 81*

Dan Powell's archive is a pull-based stream: no tape plays itself, and nothing moves until he reaches for the next one. A `VStream` works the same way. Each element comes from a task, which runs only when you pull it, and which may block or fail.

~~~admonish abstract title="VStream at a glance"
| | |
|---|---|
| **Models** | A sequence of elements, each produced by a task only when the consumer pulls it |
| **Replaces** | A loop over a paged or blocking source, or a Java `Stream` or `Iterator` built over one |
| **Effect Path** | [`VStreamPath`](../effect/path_vstream.md) |
| **Instances** | `Functor`, `Applicative`, `Monad`, `Alternative`, `Foldable`, `Traverse` |
| **Choose it when** | Elements arrive one at a time from work that may block or fail; otherwise [List](list_monad.md), or compare every type in [Choosing a Type](supported-types.md#choosing-a-type) |
~~~

~~~admonish info title="What You'll Learn"
- Read a paged source one page at a time, fetching a page only when the stream reaches it
- Say what a pull gives back, and why a stream answers some pulls with a `Skip`
- Transform elements with functions and with tasks, and collect them with a terminal operation
- Give a value in place of a failed element, or keep what was read when the source fails
- Say which thread runs a pull, and where virtual threads come in
~~~

~~~admonish example title="See Example Code"
**The code on this page is [VStreamBook.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/main/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/vstream/VStreamBook.java)**: the page includes it, so the build compiles and runs it. [VStreamBookTest.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/vstream/VStreamBookTest.java) holds what the page says each method does.
~~~

---

## Reading a feed one page at a time {#reading-a-feed-one-page-at-a-time}

An order service hands out its orders a page at a time, and each page is a call that blocks and may throw an `IOException`. In plain Java you would write a loop that fetches the next page until one comes back empty. A Java `Stream` or `Iterator` built over that loop is lazy too, but it can be read only once. The lambdas you pass to its `map` and `filter` cannot throw the feed's `IOException`. Nothing in it can give a value in place of one failed element, or keep what was read when the feed fails. A `VStream<A>` describes the sequence instead. It produces nothing until a terminal operation runs it, and you can run it as often as you like. Only a stream read from an iterator or a Java `Stream` gives its elements once, since a run uses up its source.

The code on this page uses these imports, with `Order` from the order records the [Optics](../optics/ch_intro.md) chapter introduces:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import java.io.IOException;
import java.math.BigDecimal;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Optional;
import org.higherkindedj.example.book.foundations.OrderPage;
import org.higherkindedj.example.book.optics.cast.Order;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStream;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vtask.VTask;
```

The feed gives an `OrderPage`, and a courier quotes for delivering an order:

```java
/** One page of an order feed: its number, and the orders on it. A page past the last is empty. */
public record OrderPage(int number, List<Order> orders) {

  public OrderPage {
    orders = List.copyOf(Objects.requireNonNull(orders, "orders"));
  }
}
```

```java
  // A feed serves orders a page at a time, and a courier quotes for delivering an order.
  // Each call blocks until its service answers, and may throw an IOException.
  interface OrderFeed {
    OrderPage page(int number) throws IOException;
  }

  interface Courier {
    BigDecimal quote(Order order) throws IOException;
  }

```

`VStream.unfold` builds a stream from a state, here the next page number, and a function giving a task for each state. The task gives a `VStream.Seed`, holding the element and the next state, or `Optional.empty()` to end. `flatMap` turns each page into its orders:

```java
  // Every order in the feed. A page is fetched only when the stream reaches it, and the empty
  // page past the last ends the stream
  static VStream<Order> orders(OrderFeed feed) {
    VStream<OrderPage> pages =
        VStream.unfold(
            1,
            number ->
                VTask.of(
                    () -> {
                      OrderPage page = feed.page(number);
                      return page.orders().isEmpty()
                          ? Optional.empty()
                          : Optional.of(new VStream.Seed<>(page, number + 1));
                    }));
    return pages.flatMap(page -> VStream.fromList(page.orders()));
  }

  static boolean paid(Order order) {
    return order.status() == OrderStatus.PAID;
  }

  static String customer(Order order) {
    return order.customer().name();
  }

```

`ORDERS` holds six orders, four of them paid. `PagedFeed` serves them two to a page, and records each page it is asked for. Taking the first two paid customers reads two pages, and the third is never fetched:

```java
    PagedFeed feed = new PagedFeed(ORDERS);
    VStream<String> paidCustomers =
        orders(feed).filter(VStreamBook::paid).map(VStreamBook::customer);
    List<Integer> fetchedBeforeRunning = feed.fetched();
    // []
    List<String> firstTwo = paidCustomers.take(2).toList().run();
    // [Ada, Alan]
    List<Integer> fetchedForTwo = feed.fetched();
    // [1, 2]
    List<String> everyPaid = paidCustomers.toList().run();
    // [Ada, Alan, Edsger, Ken]
    List<Integer> fetchedForAll = feed.fetched();
    // [1, 2, 1, 2, 3, 4]
```

Building `paidCustomers` fetches nothing. Each run reads the feed again from the first page. `fetched()` keeps every page asked for, so the last list holds the first run's pages 1 and 2, then the second run's pages 1 to 4, where the empty page 4 ends the stream.

## What a pull gives {#what-a-pull-gives}

`VStream` is a functional interface whose one abstract method, `pull()`, returns a `VTask<Step<A>>`. Running that task gives one of these steps:

| Step | Means |
|---|---|
| `Emit(value, tail)` | Here is an element, never null, and `tail` is the rest of the stream |
| `Skip(tail)` | No element this time; pull `tail` next |
| `Fail(error, tail)` | This element failed with `error`, and `tail` is the rest of the stream |
| `Done()` | The stream has ended |

A terminal operation is a loop that pulls, keeps each emitted value, and moves on to the tail. This is the loop that `take(2).toList()` runs over the feed:

```mermaid
%%{init: {"sequence": {"actorMargin": 20, "diagramMarginX": 10}}}%%
sequenceDiagram
    accTitle: The pulls that take two paid customers from the feed
    accDescr: toList pulls seven times. The first pull fetches page 1 and comes back as a Skip. The second gives Ada. The third reaches Grace, whom the filter rejects, and the fourth the end of page 1; both come back as Skips. The fifth fetches page 2 and is a Skip, and the sixth gives Alan. On the seventh pull, take has its two elements, so it answers Done and closes the rest of the stream.
    participant L as toList
    participant T as take(2)
    participant F as filter(paid)
    participant O as orders
    L->>O: pull 1
    Note over O: fetches page 1
    O-->>L: Skip
    L->>O: pull 2
    O-->>L: Emit Ada
    L->>O: pull 3
    O-->>F: Emit Grace
    F-->>L: Skip: Grace has not paid
    L->>O: pull 4
    O-->>L: Skip: page 1 is done
    L->>O: pull 5
    Note over O: fetches page 2
    O-->>L: Skip
    L->>O: pull 6
    O-->>L: Emit Alan
    L->>T: pull 7
    T-->>L: Done, and closes the rest
```

Each arrow ends at the operator that answers it. `orders` is the `unfold` and `flatMap`, and `map` is left out, since it passes every step straight on. Page 2 is fetched only when pull 5 reaches it, and page 3 never is. When `filter` rejects Grace, it answers `Skip` rather than pulling again itself, and `flatMap` answers `Skip` as it starts or finishes each page. The loop in `toList` does the pulling again, so however many orders a filter rejects, the stack does not grow. On the pull after its second element, `take` answers `Done` and closes the rest of the stream, so a finaliser upstream, such as a `bracket`'s release, runs.

## Creating a stream {#creating-a-stream}

| Factory | Gives |
|---|---|
| `VStream.of(a, b, …)`, `fromList(list)` | The elements given; `fromList` reads the list when pulled, without copying it |
| `range(from, to)` | The integers from `from` up to, not including, `to` |
| `iterate(seed, f)`, `generate(supplier)`, `repeat(value)` | A stream without end, which `take` or `takeWhile` limits |
| `unfold(state, f)` | An element and the next state from a task, until it gives `Optional.empty()` |
| `fromIterator(iterator)`, `fromStream(stream)` | The elements of an iterator or a Java `Stream`, which a run uses up: a later run carries on where the last one stopped |
| `defer(supplier)` | A stream built afresh each time it runs |
| `empty()`, `fail(error)` | A stream that ends at once, or fails at its first pull |
| `bracket(acquire, use, release)` | A stream that holds a resource while it is read, as [Resource-Safe Streaming](vstream_resources.md) shows |

A stream never holds a null element. `VStream.of(null)` throws at the call, and a null in a list, iterator or Java `Stream` fails the stream when that element is pulled, naming the factory, as in `VStream.fromList element cannot be null`. A function given to `map`, `zipWith` or `recover` that returns null fails it too, closing what the stream opened. [A Task Always Gives a Value](vtask_monad.md#a-task-always-gives-a-value) sets out the rule every lazy type follows.

## Shaping a stream {#shaping-a-stream}

`map` changes each element with a function, and `mapTask` with a task, which may block or fail. Here a courier quotes for each paid order:

```java
  // A quote for each paid order, asking the courier only when the stream reaches the order
  static VStream<BigDecimal> shippingQuotes(VStream<Order> orders, Courier courier) {
    return orders.filter(VStreamBook::paid).mapTask(order -> VTask.of(() -> courier.quote(order)));
  }

```

Every operator is lazy: `shippingQuotes` asks the courier nothing until the stream runs, and then only for the orders a pull reaches. `COURIER` quotes 4.99 for every order, so `fold` adds four quotes:

```java
    BigDecimal shipping =
        shippingQuotes(orders(new PagedFeed(ORDERS)), COURIER)
            .fold(BigDecimal.ZERO, BigDecimal::add)
            .run();
    // 19.96
```

The other operators are lazy in the same way:

| Operator | Gives |
|---|---|
| `filter(p)` | The elements that match `p` |
| `take(n)`, `takeWhile(p)` | The first `n` elements, or those before the first that fails `p`; the rest is then closed |
| `drop(n)`, `dropWhile(p)` | The elements after the first `n`, or from the first that fails `p` |
| `distinct()` | Each element once |
| `flatMap(f)`, `via(f)` | The elements of the stream `f` gives for each element, one stream after another |
| `concat(other)` | This stream's elements, then `other`'s, which is not pulled until this one ends |
| `zipWith(other, f)` | `f` of each pair, stopping at the shorter stream, so the other may be endless |
| `interleave(other)` | An element from each stream in turn |
| `chunk(size)` | Batches of up to `size` elements, as [Parallel Operations](vstream_parallel.md#chunking-operations) shows |

~~~admonish warning title="An endless stream needs a limit"
`iterate`, `generate` and `repeat` never end, so `toList`, `fold` and `count` on one run until an interrupt stops them, as [Running a stream](#running-a-stream) says. Limit it first with `take` or `takeWhile`. `exists` and `find` stop at the first match, so they end on an endless stream too, provided a match comes.
~~~

## Running a stream {#running-a-stream}

A terminal operation gives a `VTask`, and nothing is read until that task runs:

| Operation | Gives |
|---|---|
| `toList()` | Every element |
| `fold(identity, op)`, `foldLeft(initial, f)` | One value built from every element |
| `headOption()`, `lastOption()`, `find(p)` | The first, the last, or the first matching element, as an `Optional` |
| `count()`, `exists(p)`, `forAll(p)` | How many elements, or whether one or all of them match |
| `forEach(action)` | `Unit`, after running `action` on each element |
| `drain()` | `Unit`, after reading every element |
| `runSafe()` | Every element, or the failure, in a [Try](try_monad.md) |

The pulls run on the thread that runs the terminal operation's task. `runAsync()` starts reading at once, on a virtual thread of its own, and gives a `CompletableFuture` of every element:

```java
    VStream<Boolean> onAVirtualThread = VStream.of(1).map(_ -> Thread.currentThread().isVirtual());

    List<Boolean> fromRun = onAVirtualThread.toList().run();
    // [false]
    List<Boolean> fromRunAsync = onAVirtualThread.runAsync().join();
    // [true]
```

A terminal operation stops at an interrupt. Before it starts and between pulls, it checks the thread's interrupt status. Once that is set, it fails with an `InterruptedException` and closes what it has read, so the finalisers there run. That lets a [timeout](../effect/path_vtask.md#timeouts) stop `drain()` on an endless stream, even one whose pulls never block. The `TimeoutException` arrives once the stream is closed. A run on a thread that is already interrupted fails before its first pull, and closes nothing, since it has read nothing. An interrupt is a stop, not a failure, so `recover`, `recoverWith`, `mapError` and `onError` let it through. To give a value when a run is stopped, recover outside what stops it, as in `toList().timeout(d).recover(e -> List.of())`. A `recover` inside the timeout gives a value the timeout discards.

`VStreamPar` runs the tasks for several elements at the same time, each on a virtual thread, as [Parallel Operations](vstream_parallel.md) shows.

## When an element fails {#when-an-element-fails}

`PATCHY_COURIER` quotes as `COURIER` does, except for Edsger, whose address it does not serve. A failed task makes its element a `Fail` step. Without a `recover`, the stream fails there, and `runSafe()` holds the exception the task threw. `recover` gives a value in its place, and the stream carries on with the next order. This is the extra import:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.trymonad.Try;
```

```java
    VStream<BigDecimal> patchy = shippingQuotes(orders(new PagedFeed(ORDERS)), PATCHY_COURIER);

    Try<List<BigDecimal>> held = patchy.toList().runSafe();
    // Failure(java.io.IOException: no courier serves Edsger's address)
    List<BigDecimal> withFlatRate = patchy.recover(error -> FLAT_RATE).toList().run();
    // [4.99, 4.99, 9.99, 4.99]
```

`run()` throws the failure instead, wrapping a checked one as [Running a task](vtask_monad.md#running-a-task) describes.

When the source itself fails, there is nothing to carry on with. A feed that goes down at page 2 fails the stream there. `recoverWith` puts another stream in place of the rest, here an empty one, so the orders read before the outage are kept:

```java
    VStream<String> downAtPageTwo = orders(new PagedFeed(ORDERS, 2)).map(VStreamBook::customer);

    List<String> readBeforeTheOutage =
        downAtPageTwo.recoverWith(error -> VStream.empty()).toList().run();
    // [Ada, Grace]
```

| Operator | When the stream fails |
|---|---|
| `recover(f)` | Gives `f`'s value. After a failed task, in `mapTask` or a [parallel operation](vstream_parallel.md#when-a-task-fails), the stream carries on with the next element. After any other failure, such as a `map` function that throws or a failed source, it ends |
| `recoverWith(f)` | Reads the stream `f` gives in place of the rest |
| `mapError(f)` | Fails with the exception `f` gives instead |
| `onError(action)` | Runs `action`, then fails as before |

Each acts on a failure at whichever element it happens, not only the first. An interrupt is not a failure, as [Running a stream](#running-a-stream) says. `recoverWith`, and `recover` when it ends the stream, close the stream they stop reading, so a finaliser upstream runs.

~~~admonish note title="Operators between mapTask and recover"
When `recover` carries on after a failed `mapTask` task, the operators between the two apply to the elements after it as well. The failed element keeps its place in the stream. In `mapTask(quote).filter(cheap).take(3).recover(f)`, the `filter` tests the quotes after it, and `take` counts it as one of its three.
~~~

## Methods {#methods}

| Method | What it does |
|---|---|
| `VStream.of`, `fromList`, `range`, `iterate`, `unfold`, `defer` | Describe a stream; nothing is read until it runs |
| `map(f)`, `mapTask(f)`, `flatMap(f)`, `via(f)` | Change each element with a function or a task, or put a stream in its place |
| `filter(p)`, `take(n)`, `takeWhile(p)`, `drop(n)`, `dropWhile(p)`, `distinct()` | Choose which elements go on |
| `concat(other)`, `zipWith(other, f)`, `interleave(other)` | Combine two streams |
| `recover(f)`, `recoverWith(f)`, `mapError(f)`, `onError(action)` | Handle a failure |
| `onFinalize(task)`, `bracket(acquire, use, release)` | Run cleanup however the stream ends |
| `toList()`, `fold`, `headOption()`, `find(p)`, `exists(p)`, `count()`, `forEach(action)`, `runSafe()` | Give a `VTask` that runs the stream |
| `runAsync()` | Start running the stream on a virtual thread, giving a `CompletableFuture` |
| `pull()` | Give a task for the next step |
| `VSTREAM.widen(stream)`, `VSTREAM.narrow(kind)` | Move a stream in and out of `Kind` |

## Working with Kind {#working-with-kind}

Generic code, written once for any container, takes `VStream` through its instances. `Instances.monad(vstream())` gives a `VStreamMonad`, whose `flatMap` puts a stream in place of each element. These are the extra imports:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import static org.higherkindedj.hkt.instances.Witnesses.vstream;
import static org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStreamKindHelper.VSTREAM;

import org.higherkindedj.hkt.Kind;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.Monad;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.instances.Instances;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStreamKind;
```

```java
    Monad<VStreamKind.Witness> streams = Instances.monad(vstream());

    Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, String> customers = VSTREAM.widen(VStream.of("Ada", "Alan"));
    Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, String> greetings =
        streams.flatMap(name -> streams.of("Dear " + name), customers);
    List<String> letters = VSTREAM.narrow(greetings).toList().run();
    // [Dear Ada, Dear Alan]
```

Widening allocates nothing: `VStream` implements `VStreamKind` itself, so `widen` hands back the same stream, and `narrow` checks it is one. [HKT and Type Classes](vstream_hkt.md) covers the other instances, and what `ap` and `orElse` mean for a stream.

## When to use `VStream` {#choosing-the-right-streaming-tool}

| You need | Use |
|---|---|
| Elements one at a time from work that blocks or fails, such as a paged API | `VStream`, on a [`VStreamPath`](../effect/path_vstream.md) in application code |
| The tasks for several elements at the same time | [Parallel Operations](vstream_parallel.md) |
| A connection or file held open while the stream is read | [Resource-Safe Streaming](vstream_resources.md) |
| Values already in memory | [List](list_monad.md), or a Java [Stream](stream_monad.md) for a pipeline read once |
| Values that cannot fail, in a pipeline you run more than once | `StreamPath`, in the [Effect Path cheatsheet](../cheatsheet.md) |
| Elements from a `Flow.Publisher`, or handed to one | [Advanced Features](vstream_advanced.md) |
| One value from work that may block | [VTask](vtask_monad.md) |

~~~admonish info title="Hands-On Learning"
Practise `VStream` in [TutorialVStream](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/tutorial/concurrency/TutorialVStream.java) (11 exercises).
~~~

**Benchmarks:** `VStreamBenchmark` measures building streams, their operators and terminal operations, and compares them with Java's `Stream`. [Performance](vstream_performance.md) says what it measures and how to run it, and [Benchmarks](../benchmarks.md) covers every benchmark.

---

**Next:** [HKT and Type Classes](vstream_hkt.md)
