<!-- description: VStream's type class instances in Java: pass a stream to generic code over Functor, Monad, Alternative or Traverse, and see what ap and orElse do. -->

# VStream and the Type Classes

_Pass a stream to code written once for any container, over `Functor`, `Monad`, `Alternative` or `Traverse`._

~~~admonish info title="What You'll Learn"
- Write a function once over `Functor`, and use it with a stream, a list and a `Maybe`
- Get `VStream`'s instances from `Instances`, and know which class each one is
- Say what `ap` and `orElse` do with a stream, and why `ap` suits only a finite one
- Fold and traverse a finite stream, knowing that both read it as soon as they are called
- Check that the instances keep their laws, with `hkj-test`
~~~

~~~admonish example title="See Example Code"
**The code on this page is [VStreamHktBook.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/main/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/vstream/VStreamHktBook.java)**: the page includes it, so the build compiles and runs it. [VStreamHktBookTest.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/vstream/VStreamHktBookTest.java) holds what the page says each instance does, and checks the laws.
~~~

---

Most code uses a stream through its own methods, or on a [`VStreamPath`](../effect/path_vstream.md). This page is for code written once for any container, such as a helper over `Functor<F>` that a stream, a list and a `Maybe` all pass through. The [Foundations](../hkts/foundations_intro.md) introduction's "Which level am I working at?" tip says which you need.

## One function for every container {#one-function-for-every-container}

The code on this page uses these imports, with the order feed and the orders from the [VStream](vstream.md#reading-a-feed-one-page-at-a-time) page:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import static org.higherkindedj.hkt.instances.Witnesses.list;
import static org.higherkindedj.hkt.instances.Witnesses.maybe;
import static org.higherkindedj.hkt.instances.Witnesses.vstream;
import static org.higherkindedj.hkt.list.ListKindHelper.LIST;
import static org.higherkindedj.hkt.maybe.MaybeKindHelper.MAYBE;
import static org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStreamKindHelper.VSTREAM;

import java.math.BigDecimal;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.function.Function;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.Alternative;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.Applicative;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.Functor;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.Kind;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.Monoids;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.Traverse;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.TypeArity;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.WitnessArity;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.instances.Instances;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.maybe.Maybe;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.maybe.MaybeKind;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStream;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStreamKind;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStreamTraverse;
```

`customers` is written once, for any `Functor`, and names no container:

```java
  // Written once, for any Functor: the customer of each order
  static <F extends WitnessArity<TypeArity.Unary>> Kind<F, String> customers(
      Functor<F> functor, Kind<F, Order> orders) {
    return functor.map(VStreamBook::customer, orders);
  }

```

A stream read from the feed, a list and a `Maybe` all pass through it. `Instances.functor` gives each container's `Functor` from its token, such as `vstream()`:

```java
    Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, String> streamed =
        customers(Instances.functor(vstream()), VSTREAM.widen(orders(new PagedFeed(ORDERS))));
    List<String> fromTheFeed = VSTREAM.narrow(streamed).toList().run();
    // [Ada, Grace, Alan, Edsger, Barbara, Ken]
    List<String> fromAList = LIST.narrow(customers(Instances.functor(list()), LIST.widen(ORDERS)));
    // [Ada, Grace, Alan, Edsger, Barbara, Ken]
    Maybe<String> fromAMaybe =
        MAYBE.narrow(
            customers(Instances.functor(maybe()), MAYBE.widen(Maybe.just(ORDERS.getFirst()))));
    // Just(Ada)
```

For the stream, `map` stays lazy: the feed is read only when `toList()` runs.

## The instances {#the-instances}

Generic code takes a container through its instances, as [Working with Kind](vstream.md#working-with-kind) on the VStream page shows for `Monad`. These are `VStream`'s:

| Type class | Get it with | Instance |
|---|---|---|
| `Functor`, `Applicative`, `Monad`, `Alternative` | `Instances.functor(vstream())`, or `applicative`, `monad` or `alternative` | `VStreamAlternative.INSTANCE`, which extends `VStreamMonad` |
| `Foldable`, `Traverse` | `VStreamTraverse.INSTANCE` | `VStreamTraverse` |

All four lookups give that one object, viewed as each type class. `VStream` has no `MonadZero` and no `Selective`, so `Instances.monadZero(vstream())` compiles but throws a `ClassCastException`. [The hierarchy](../functional/ch_intro.md#the-hierarchy) shows how the type classes extend each other.

## `ap` pairs every function with every value {#ap-pairs-every-function-with-every-value}

[`Applicative`](../functional/applicative.md)'s `ap` applies each function in the first stream to every value in the second, each function in turn, as `List`'s `ap` does. Two delivery services for the first two paid customers give four offers:

```java
    PagedFeed feed = new PagedFeed(ORDERS);
    Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, String> firstTwoPaid =
        VSTREAM.widen(orders(feed).filter(VStreamBook::paid).map(VStreamBook::customer).take(2));
    Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, Function<String, String>> services =
        VSTREAM.widen(
            VStream.<Function<String, String>>of(
                name -> "standard for " + name, name -> "express for " + name));

    Applicative<VStreamKind.Witness> applicative = Instances.applicative(vstream());
    List<String> offers = VSTREAM.narrow(applicative.ap(services, firstTwoPaid)).toList().run();
    // [standard for Ada, standard for Alan, express for Ada, express for Alan]
    List<Integer> fetched = feed.fetched();
    // [1, 2]
```

The feed was read once, though two functions used its values. Each run of the result reads the value stream to its end before it gives its first element, then holds those values for every function.

~~~admonish warning title="ap reads every value first"
On an endless value stream, `ap` never gives an element. To pair the elements of two streams one with one, use `zipWith`, from [Shaping a stream](vstream.md#shaping-a-stream).
~~~

## `orElse` joins two streams {#orelse-joins-two-streams}

For a stream, `orElse` is `concat`: it gives every element of the first, then every element of the second, and builds the second only once the first has ended. `empty()` is a stream with no elements:

```java
    Alternative<VStreamKind.Witness> alternative = Instances.alternative(vstream());
    Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, String> london = VSTREAM.widen(VStream.of("Ada", "Alan"));
    Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, String> edinburgh = VSTREAM.widen(VStream.of("Edsger", "Ken"));

    List<String> both = VSTREAM.narrow(alternative.orElse(london, () -> edinburgh)).toList().run();
    // [Ada, Alan, Edsger, Ken]
    List<String> londonOnly =
        VSTREAM.narrow(alternative.orElse(london, () -> alternative.empty())).toList().run();
    // [Ada, Alan]
```

This is not a fallback. `Maybe`'s `orElse` uses its second value only when the first is `Nothing`, while a stream's joins the second even when the first has elements. [One method, two meanings](../functional/alternative.md#one-method-two-meanings) sets the two side by side. `guard(true)` gives a stream of one element, and `guard(false)` an empty one.

## Folding and traversing a finite stream {#folding-and-traversing-a-finite-stream}

`foldMap`, from [Foldable](../functional/foldable_and_traverse.md), maps each element to a value of a [`Monoid`](../functional/semigroup_and_monoid.md), then combines them all. Here each paid order's value is added up, at 40.00 an order:

```java
  // What an order is worth: each line's price times its quantity
  static BigDecimal value(Order order) {
    return order.lines().stream()
        .map(line -> line.price().multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(line.quantity())))
        .reduce(BigDecimal.ZERO, BigDecimal::add);
  }

```

```java
    Traverse<VStreamKind.Witness> traverse = VStreamTraverse.INSTANCE;
    Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, Order> paidOrders =
        VSTREAM.widen(orders(new PagedFeed(ORDERS)).filter(VStreamBook::paid));

    BigDecimal paidValue =
        traverse.foldMap(Monoids.bigDecimalAddition(), VStreamHktBook::value, paidOrders);
    // 160.00
```

`traverse` applies an effectful function to each element, and gives one effect holding a stream of the results. `POSTCODES` holds a postcode for each paid customer, and none for Grace or Barbara. So with `Maybe`, `traverse` gives `Just` over the paid orders, and `Nothing` over every order:

```java
    Function<Order, Kind<MaybeKind.Witness, String>> postcode =
        order -> MAYBE.widen(Maybe.fromNullable(POSTCODES.get(customer(order))));

    Kind<MaybeKind.Witness, Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, String>> forThePaid =
        traverse.traverse(Instances.applicative(maybe()), postcode, paidOrders);
    Maybe<List<String>> labels =
        MAYBE.narrow(forThePaid).map(stream -> VSTREAM.narrow(stream).toList().run());
    // Just([N1 9GU, M13 9PL, CB2 1TN, EH8 9YL])

    Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, Order> everyOrder = VSTREAM.widen(orders(new PagedFeed(ORDERS)));
    Maybe<Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, String>> forEveryOrder =
        MAYBE.narrow(traverse.traverse(Instances.applicative(maybe()), postcode, everyOrder));
    // Nothing
```

~~~admonish warning title="foldMap and traverse run the stream when they are called"
Neither gives a `VTask`. Each reads the whole stream there and then, on the calling thread, and `traverse` holds every element while it works. So neither returns on an endless stream: bound it first with `take` or `takeWhile`. A checked exception from the stream reaches the caller inside a `VTaskExecutionException`, as [`VTask.run()`](vtask_monad.md#running-a-task) hands one over. In application code, prefer the stream's own `fold`, which gives a `VTask`, as [Running a stream](vstream.md#running-a-stream) shows.
~~~

## Laws {#laws}

The book's own test checks that `VStream`'s instances keep their laws, with the law checkers that `hkj-test` ships.

~~~admonish note title="Laws"
Two streams count as the same when a run of each gives the same elements in the same order. On that measure, `VStream`'s instances keep the laws of [Functor](../functional/functor.md#two-laws), [Applicative](../functional/applicative.md#four-laws) and [Monad](../functional/monad.md#three-laws), and `Traverse`'s identity law, the one [`hkj-test`](../tooling/test_assertions.md#adding-the-dependency) checks. The test also checks that `widen` then `narrow` gives back the stream it started with. It uses the orders from the VStream page, with these imports and JUnit's:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import static org.higherkindedj.hkt.instances.Witnesses.vstream;
import static org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStreamKindHelper.VSTREAM;

import java.util.function.BiPredicate;
import java.util.function.Function;
import org.higherkindedj.example.book.optics.cast.Customer;
import org.higherkindedj.example.book.optics.cast.LineItem;
import org.higherkindedj.example.book.optics.cast.Order;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.Kind;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.Monad;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.assertions.KindEquivalence;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.instances.Instances;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.laws.ApplicativeLaws;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.laws.FunctorLaws;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.laws.KindHelperLaws;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.laws.MonadLaws;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.laws.TraverseLaws;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStream;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStreamKind;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStreamTraverse;
```

```java
  // Two streams are the same when a run of each gives the same elements in the same order
  private static final BiPredicate<Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, ?>, Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, ?>>
      SAME = KindEquivalence.byEqualsAfter(kind -> VSTREAM.narrow(kind).toList().run());

  @Test
  @DisplayName("VStream's instances keep the Functor, Applicative, Monad and Traverse laws")
  void laws() {
    Monad<VStreamKind.Witness> monad = Instances.monad(vstream());
    Order ada = ORDERS.getFirst();
    Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, Order> orders = VSTREAM.widen(VStream.fromList(ORDERS));
    Function<Order, Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, LineItem>> lines =
        order -> VSTREAM.widen(VStream.fromList(order.lines()));
    Function<LineItem, Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, String>> skus =
        line -> VSTREAM.widen(VStream.of(line.sku(), line.sku() + " spare"));
    Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, Function<Order, Customer>> customer =
        VSTREAM.widen(VStream.of(Order::customer));
    Kind<VStreamKind.Witness, Function<Customer, String>> name =
        VSTREAM.widen(VStream.of(Customer::name));

    FunctorLaws.assertIdentity(monad, orders, SAME);
    FunctorLaws.assertComposition(monad, orders, Order::customer, Customer::name, SAME);
    ApplicativeLaws.assertIdentity(monad, orders, SAME);
    ApplicativeLaws.assertHomomorphism(monad, ada, Order::customer, SAME);
    ApplicativeLaws.assertInterchange(monad, customer, ada, SAME);
    ApplicativeLaws.assertComposition(monad, name, customer, orders, SAME);
    MonadLaws.assertLeftIdentity(monad, ada, lines, SAME);
    MonadLaws.assertRightIdentity(monad, orders, SAME);
    MonadLaws.assertAssociativity(monad, orders, lines, skus, SAME);
    TraverseLaws.assertIdentity(VStreamTraverse.INSTANCE, orders, SAME);
    KindHelperLaws.assertRoundTrip(VStream.fromList(ORDERS), VSTREAM::widen, VSTREAM::narrow);
  }

```

Checking `Alternative`'s laws with `hkj-test` is not supported yet. The library's own tests check that `empty()` is an identity on both sides of `orElse`, and that `orElse` is associative.
~~~

## How `VStream` compares {#how-vstream-compares}

| | `VStream` | `List` | Java `Stream` | `Maybe` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `of(a)` | A stream of one element | `[a]` | A stream of one element | `Just(a)` |
| `ap` | Every function over every value, the values read at each run | Every function over every value | Every function over every value, the values read when `ap` is called | `Just` when both are |
| `orElse` | Both, joined | Both, joined | Both, joined | The first `Just` |
| `foldMap` | Reads the stream when called | Folds the list | Reads the stream when called | The value's, or the empty value |
| Each element | Produced by a task when pulled | Already in memory | Produced when pulled, once only | Already in memory |

~~~admonish info title="Hands-On Learning"
Practise `VStream`'s instances in [TutorialVStreamHKT](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/tutorial/concurrency/TutorialVStreamHKT.java) (10 exercises).
~~~

---

**Previous:** [VStream](vstream.md)
**Next:** [Parallel Operations](vstream_parallel.md)

<!-- Source: monads/vstream_parallel.md -->

<!-- description: Run several VStream elements' tasks at once with VStreamPar: in order or as each completes, in batches, or merged from several streams. -->

# Parallel Operations: Several Elements at Once

_Run the tasks for several elements at the same time, batch elements for a bulk call, and merge streams._

~~~admonish info title="What You'll Learn"
- Run several elements' tasks at once, up to a limit you set, keeping their order or taking each result as it arrives
- Say how far each operation reads ahead of the consumer, and which thread reads the source
- Choose a concurrency, and keep going when one element's task fails
- Batch elements for a call that takes several at a time
- Merge streams read at the same time
~~~

~~~admonish example title="See Example Code"
**The code on this page is [VStreamParBook.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/main/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/vstream/VStreamParBook.java)**: the page includes it, so the build compiles and runs it. [VStreamParBookTest.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/vstream/VStreamParBookTest.java) holds what the page says each operation does.
~~~

---

## Several quotes at once {#several-quotes-at-once}

`mapTask` runs one element's task at a time, so four courier quotes that each take a second take four seconds. `VStreamPar` runs several at once, each on a virtual thread.

~~~admonish warning title="A preview API"
`VStreamPar` is built on `StructuredTaskScope`, a preview feature in Java 25, so code that names it needs `--enable-preview` to compile and to run. `VStreamPath`'s `parEvalMap` and `parEvalMapUnordered` compile without it and need it when they run, as the [VTask](vtask_monad.md#on-a-vtaskpath) page explains for `VTaskPath`. The [HKJ Gradle plugin](../tooling/gradle_plugin.md) adds it by default.
~~~

The code on this page uses the order feed and the `Courier` from the [VStream](vstream.md#reading-a-feed-one-page-at-a-time) page, with these imports:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import java.io.IOException;
import java.math.BigDecimal;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Map;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.effect.Path;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.trymonad.Try;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStream;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStreamPar;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vtask.VTask;
```

The paid orders are the stream to quote for, and `ZONED_COURIER` quotes by where each customer lives, so each quote shows which order it belongs to:

```java
  static VStream<Order> paidOrders() {
    return orders(new PagedFeed(ORDERS)).filter(VStreamBook::paid);
  }

```

```java
  // A courier whose quote depends on where the customer lives
  static final Map<String, BigDecimal> RATES =
      Map.of(
          "Ada", new BigDecimal("4.99"),
          "Alan", new BigDecimal("6.49"),
          "Edsger", new BigDecimal("3.99"),
          "Ken", new BigDecimal("5.49"));

  static final Courier ZONED_COURIER = order -> RATES.get(customer(order));

```

`parEvalMap(stream, concurrency, f)` asks for up to `concurrency` quotes at once, and gives them in the orders' order:

```java
    // At most four quotes asked for at once, and given in the orders' order
    VStream<BigDecimal> quotes =
        VStreamPar.parEvalMap(paidOrders(), 4, order -> VTask.of(() -> ZONED_COURIER.quote(order)));

    List<BigDecimal> inOrder = quotes.toList().run();
    // [4.99, 6.49, 3.99, 5.49]
```

It works a batch at a time. A pull reads up to `concurrency` elements from the source, forks a task for each, and waits for every task in the batch. It answers that pull with the first result, and answers the pulls after it from the rest of the batch:

```mermaid
%%{init: {"sequence": {"actorMargin": 20, "diagramMarginX": 10}}}%%
sequenceDiagram
    accTitle: How parEvalMap answers the pulls for four quotes
    accDescr: The first pull reads four paid orders from the source, forks a quote task for each, and waits for all four before it answers with the first quote. The next three pulls are answered from the batch. The fifth pull starts the next batch: it reads the source again, finds its end, and answers Done.
    participant L as toList
    participant P as parEvalMap(4)
    participant S as paid orders
    participant Q as quote tasks
    L->>P: pull 1
    P->>S: read four orders
    P->>Q: fork four quotes
    Q-->>P: all four quotes
    P-->>L: Emit 4.99
    L->>P: pulls 2 to 4
    P-->>L: Emit 6.49, 3.99, 5.49
    L->>P: pull 5
    P->>S: read
    S-->>P: Done
    P-->>L: Done
```

The arrows to the right are pulls and forks, and the dashed arrows back are what each gives. A batch's results wait for its slowest task. The source is read again only after every result of the batch has been taken. The [tail](vstream.md#what-a-pull-gives) of the batch's last result is the next batch, so pull 5 reads the source and finds its end.

The source is read on the thread that pulls, and each task runs on a virtual thread of its own, which sees the caller's `ScopedValue` bindings. A `concurrency` of 1 runs the tasks one at a time, as `mapTask` does, but each on a virtual thread.

## In order, or as each arrives {#in-order-or-as-each-arrives}

When the order of the results does not matter, `parEvalMapUnordered` gives each one as its task completes, so a slow quote holds back only itself:

```java
    // Each quote given as soon as the courier answers it
    VStream<String> arriving =
        VStreamPar.parEvalMapUnordered(
            paidOrders(),
            4,
            order -> VTask.of(() -> customer(order) + " " + ZONED_COURIER.quote(order)));

    List<String> asTheyArrived = arriving.toList().run();
    List<String> sorted = asTheyArrived.stream().sorted().toList();
    // [Ada 4.99, Alan 6.49, Edsger 3.99, Ken 5.49]
```

The quotes arrive in whatever order the courier answers, so the example sorts them to show they are all there.

| | `parEvalMap` | `parEvalMapUnordered` |
|---|---|---|
| Order of the results | The elements' order | The order the tasks complete |
| A slow task holds back | Its whole batch, and the next | Only its own result |
| Reads ahead of the consumer | A batch of up to `concurrency` elements | Up to `concurrency` elements whose results are not yet pulled |
| Reads the source on | The thread that pulls | A virtual thread of its own |
| Tasks see the caller's `ScopedValue` bindings | Yes | No |

`parEvalMapUnordered` starts the next element's task each time you take a result. If you stop reading part-way, close the tail you hold, or the run's thread is left waiting. `take` and the other operations that stop early close it for you.

`parEvalFlatMap(stream, concurrency, f)` builds the stream `f` gives for each element, up to `concurrency` at once through `parEvalMap`, then reads those streams one after another, in order. Closing it closes each stream it built but never reached, so their finalisers run when a `take` stops early or a failure ends the stream first.

## Choosing a concurrency {#choosing-a-concurrency}

For tasks that wait on I/O, such as calls to another service, set `concurrency` to the number of calls that service accepts at once. Virtual threads make the waiting cheap, so the service's limit is the one that counts. For tasks that compute, more than `Runtime.getRuntime().availableProcessors()` gains nothing, since that is how many virtual threads run at once by default. Each stage of a pipeline takes its own `concurrency`, so a stage that downloads can run eight at once and the stage that uploads after it two.

## When a task fails {#when-a-task-fails}

A task that fails makes its element a failure in its place, as a failed `mapTask` task does, and the other tasks run on. `PATCHY_COURIER`, from [When an element fails](vstream.md#when-an-element-fails), quotes 4.99 for every order but cannot quote for Edsger, and `FLAT_RATE` is 9.99:

```java
    VStream<BigDecimal> patchy =
        VStreamPar.parEvalMap(
            paidOrders(), 4, order -> VTask.of(() -> PATCHY_COURIER.quote(order)));

    Try<List<BigDecimal>> held = patchy.toList().runSafe();
    // Failure(java.io.IOException: no courier serves Edsger's address)
    List<BigDecimal> recovered = patchy.recover(error -> FLAT_RATE).toList().run();
    // [4.99, 4.99, 9.99, 4.99]
```

Without a `recover`, the stream fails with the courier's own exception when it reaches Edsger's quote. A `recover` after the parallel operation gives a value in its place, and the stream goes on with the next quote. `parEvalMapUnordered` hands on a task's failure as the task completes, as it does a result. In `parEvalFlatMap`, a call of `f` that fails is a failed task, so `recover` gives one value in place of the stream that call would have built.

Two failures still end the stream. A function that throws, rather than returning a task that fails, ends it as it would `mapTask`, so do the work inside the task, as `VTask.of` does. A task that fails with an `InterruptedException` ends it at once: the tasks still running are cancelled, and `parEvalMap` hands on none of that batch's results. A `recover` after it lets the interrupt through, as [Running a stream](vstream.md#running-a-stream) says, so the run fails with it.

A failed element from further up is handed on in the same way. When a `mapTask` task fails before the parallel operation, its element passes through as a failure, so a `recover` after the parallel operation gives a value in its place and the stream goes on. `parEvalMap`, `parEvalMapUnordered` and `parEvalFlatMap` all do this, while a merge of several streams ends there. Any other failure further up, such as a `map` that throws, ends the stream, after the elements read before it.

## Chunking operations {#chunking-operations}

`chunk(size)` groups elements into batches of up to `size`, and `chunkWhile` starts a new batch wherever a test fails between neighbours:

```java
    List<List<String>> pairs =
        paidOrders()
            .chunk(2)
            .map(batch -> batch.map(VStreamBook::customer).toJavaList())
            .toList()
            .run();
    // [[Ada, Alan], [Edsger, Ken]]
    List<List<String>> byStatus =
        orders(new PagedFeed(ORDERS))
            .chunkWhile((earlier, later) -> earlier.status() == later.status())
            .map(batch -> batch.map(VStreamBook::customer).toJavaList())
            .toList()
            .run();
    // [[Ada], [Grace], [Alan, Edsger], [Barbara], [Ken]]
```

Each batch is a [`NonEmptyList`](nonemptylist_monad.md), since a chunk is given only once it holds an element, so `head()` and `reduce` on it always have a value. The last chunk may be shorter, and an empty stream gives no chunks. A `size` larger than the stream, even `Integer.MAX_VALUE`, gives one chunk of every element, without reserving room for the rest. A `parEvalMap` `concurrency` that large reads the whole stream as one batch in the same way.

A batch suits a call that takes several elements at once, such as a warehouse that packs a list of orders:

```java
  // A warehouse packs several orders in one call, which blocks until it answers
  interface Warehouse {
    List<String> pack(List<Order> orders) throws IOException;
  }

```

`chunk` followed by `parEvalMap` makes one call for each batch, with up to `concurrency` calls at once. The warehouse's `pack` blocks and can throw, so each call runs as a task. `mapChunked(size, f)` suits a function that cannot fail and does not block: it applies `f` to each batch and flattens the lists it gives:

```java
    // Two orders to a call, and two calls at once
    VStream<List<String>> boxes =
        VStreamPar.parEvalMap(
            paidOrders().chunk(2), 2, batch -> VTask.of(() -> WAREHOUSE.pack(batch.toJavaList())));

    List<List<String>> packed = boxes.toList().run();
    // [[box for Ada, box for Alan], [box for Edsger, box for Ken]]
    List<String> labels =
        paidOrders()
            .mapChunked(2, batch -> batch.map(order -> "label for " + customer(order)).toJavaList())
            .toList()
            .run();
    // [label for Ada, label for Alan, label for Edsger, label for Ken]
```

## Merging streams {#merge-concurrent-multi-source-consumption}

`VStreamPar.merge` reads several streams at the same time, each on a virtual thread of its own, and gives each element as it arrives from any of them. The sources' threads do not see the caller's `ScopedValue` bindings:

```java
    VStream<String> london = orders(new PagedFeed(ORDERS.subList(0, 3))).map(VStreamBook::customer);
    VStream<String> edinburgh =
        orders(new PagedFeed(ORDERS.subList(3, 6))).map(VStreamBook::customer);

    List<String> everyCustomer = VStreamPar.merge(london, edinburgh).toList().run();
    List<String> alphabetical = everyCustomer.stream().sorted().toList();
    // [Ada, Alan, Barbara, Edsger, Grace, Ken]
```

The order depends on which feed answers first, so the example sorts the names. When the consumer stops early, with `take(n)` or `headOption()` for example, the merge is closed. It interrupts any source still being read and waits for the sources' threads to stop. Then it closes each source that had not finished, so each source's finalisers run. A source failing stops the merge in the same way. A source finaliser that fails while the merge closes is dropped, since the merge has already stopped.

~~~admonish warning title="merge reads ahead without a limit"
A merge of several streams reads each source as fast as the source gives elements, and holds the elements you have not pulled yet without a limit. While the consumer is slower than its sources, the backlog can grow to every element they give, so a fast endless source fills memory, and so can a large finite one. Bound an endless source with `take` or `takeWhile` before merging it, and merge finite sources only when all they give fits in memory. A merge of one stream is that stream, so it reads no further ahead than that stream does.
~~~

## How far each operation reads ahead {#how-far-each-operation-reads-ahead}

A `VStream` is read by pulling, so a consumer that stops pulling stops the reading. Each operation reads only so far ahead of it:

| Operation | Reads ahead of the consumer |
|---|---|
| `parEvalMap`, `parEvalFlatMap` | One batch of up to `concurrency` elements, at a pull |
| `parEvalMapUnordered` | Up to `concurrency` elements whose results are not yet pulled |
| `chunk(size)`, `mapChunked(size, f)` | One batch of up to `size` elements |
| `merge` of several streams | Every element its sources give, without a limit |

Those bounds are why a `VStream` needs no backpressure protocol, the signal by which a consumer tells a producer how much it can take, of the kind Project Reactor's streams use. When the consumer is slow, the operation waits, and a waiting virtual thread holds no platform thread. A merge of several streams is the exception, as its warning says.

## On a `VStreamPath` {#on-a-vstreampath}

[`VStreamPath`](../effect/path_vstream.md) has `parEvalMap`, `parEvalMapUnordered`, `chunk` and `mapChunked` too:

```java
    List<BigDecimal> viaPath =
        Path.vstream(paidOrders())
            .parEvalMap(4, order -> VTask.of(() -> ZONED_COURIER.quote(order)))
            .toList()
            .unsafeRun();
    // [4.99, 6.49, 3.99, 5.49]
```

## Collecting the results {#collecting-the-results}

`toList()` collects what a parallel operation gives, as each example on this page does. The work runs in parallel inside the tasks `parEvalMap`'s function returns; collecting is reading the stream, one pull at a time, so it cannot be parallel itself.

`VStreamPar.parCollect(stream, batchSize)`, `VStreamPath.parCollect` and `PathOps.parCollectVStream` are deprecated for removal in 0.7.0. Each gives what `toList()` gives. The [0.7.0 deprecation recipe](../tooling/openrewrite.md#070-deprecation-migration) rewrites each call to `toList()`, and marks any call it cannot rewrite safely.

## Methods {#methods}

| Method | What it does |
|---|---|
| `VStreamPar.parEvalMap(stream, concurrency, f)` | Runs up to `concurrency` tasks at once, a batch at a time, and keeps the elements' order |
| `VStreamPar.parEvalMapUnordered(stream, concurrency, f)` | Runs up to `concurrency` tasks at once, and gives each result as its task completes |
| `VStreamPar.parEvalFlatMap(stream, concurrency, f)` | Builds up to `concurrency` of `f`'s streams at once, and reads them in order |
| `VStreamPar.merge(streams)`, `VStreamPar.merge(first, second)` | Reads every stream at once, and gives each element as it arrives |
| `stream.chunk(size)`, `stream.chunkWhile(test)` | Batches of up to `size` elements, or of neighbours that pass the test |
| `stream.mapChunked(size, f)` | Applies `f` to each batch, and flattens the lists it gives |

## When to use parallel operations {#when-to-use-them}

| You need | Use |
|---|---|
| A task for each element, several at once, results in order | `parEvalMap` |
| The same, each result as soon as it is ready | `parEvalMapUnordered` |
| One call for several elements | `chunk`, then `parEvalMap` |
| Elements from several sources as they arrive | `merge`, with endless sources bounded |
| One task at a time, on the caller's thread | `mapTask`, on the [VStream](vstream.md#shaping-a-stream) page |
| A few independent tasks, not a stream | [Structured Concurrency](vtask_scope.md) |

~~~admonish info title="Hands-On Learning"
Practise parallel streams in [TutorialVStreamParallel](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/tutorial/concurrency/TutorialVStreamParallel.java) (8 exercises).
~~~

**Benchmarks:** `VStreamBenchmark` measures `parEvalMap`, `parEvalMapUnordered` and the chunking operations. [Performance](vstream_performance.md) says what it measures and how to run it, and [Benchmarks](../benchmarks.md) covers every benchmark.

---

**Next:** [Performance](vstream_performance.md)

<!-- description: Where a VStream's time goes: what a pull costs, where threads start, when chunking pays, and how to measure it with JMH. -->

# Performance: Where a Stream's Time Goes

_Each element costs a call on your own thread, and threads start only where you ask for them._

~~~admonish info title="What You'll Learn"
- Say what each pull costs, and on which thread it runs
- Know which operations start virtual threads, and when that cost pays for itself
- Say what chunking saves, and what it does not
- Choose between a `VStream` and a Java `Stream` for a pipeline
- Run `VStreamBenchmark`, and read what it measures
~~~

~~~admonish example title="See Example Code"
[VStreamPerformanceBookTest.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/vstream/VStreamPerformanceBookTest.java) holds what this page says about where each cost falls, and [VStreamBenchmark](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-benchmarks/src/jmh/java/org/higherkindedj/benchmarks/VStreamBenchmark.java) measures the costs.
~~~

---

## What a pull costs {#what-a-pull-costs}

Each element reaches the consumer through a pull: a call that returns a `VTask`, which gives a step when it runs. A terminal operation run with `run()` or `runSafe()` makes every pull on that thread, and starts none. Each operator between the source and the consumer adds a task, a step and a call for every element: nanoseconds, where starting a thread takes microseconds. [What a pull gives](vstream.md#what-a-pull-gives) traces the pulls of a small pipeline.

Building a pipeline runs nothing. `map`, `filter`, `flatMap` and `mapTask` each wrap the stream they are given, and their functions run only when a terminal operation pulls.

## Against a Java `Stream` {#against-a-java-stream}

A Java `Stream` pushes each element through its operations in one loop, and makes no task or step for each element. So for work on data already in memory, such as mapping a list, it is faster than a `VStream`. A `VStream` earns its cost when the work for an element waits: a call to another service takes milliseconds, and a pull's nanoseconds vanish beside it.

| | `VStream` | Java `Stream` |
|---|---|---|
| Runs | Each time its terminal task runs | Once, when its terminal operation is called |
| An element | Pulled by the consumer | Pushed through the pipeline |
| An effect for an element | `mapTask`, on the thread that reads the stream | By hand, inside a function |
| A failure | A failed `mapTask` task gives a `Fail` step, which `recover` replaces and reads on from | An exception, which ends the pipeline |
| Cleanup | `bracket` and `onFinalize`, however the run ends | `onClose`, when the stream is closed |
| Parallel work | `VStreamPar`, a bounded number of tasks at once | `parallel()`, on the common pool |
| Suits | Work that waits, effects, and resources | Work on data in memory |

A stream read from an iterator or a Java `Stream` is used up by its first run, and a `bracket` opens its resource again for each run. [Advanced Features](vstream_advanced.md) covers a stream read from a `Flow.Publisher`.

## Where threads start {#where-threads-start}

| Operation | Threads it starts |
|---|---|
| `map`, `filter`, `flatMap`, `mapTask`, `take`, `chunk`, and every terminal operation run with `run()` or `runSafe()` | None: each runs on the thread that runs the terminal operation |
| `runAsync()` | One virtual thread, for the whole run |
| `VStreamPar.parEvalMap` | A virtual thread for each task |
| `VStreamPar.parEvalFlatMap` | A virtual thread for each task that builds a stream; the streams it builds are read on the reading thread |
| `VStreamPar.parEvalMapUnordered` | A virtual thread that reads the source, and one for each task |
| `VStreamPar.merge` of several streams | A virtual thread for each source, one that gathers them, and one that closes them when the consumer stops early |
| `VStreamReactive.toPublisher` | Virtual threads that pull for its subscribers |

Starting a virtual thread and waiting for it costs far more than a pull. So a parallel operation pays when each task waits, on I/O or a slow service. For a task that only computes something small, `map` or `mapTask` is faster. `parEvalMap` also waits for the slowest task in each batch; when the order of the results does not matter, `parEvalMapUnordered` lets each one go as its task completes, as [Parallel Operations](vstream_parallel.md) shows.

## What chunking saves {#what-chunking-saves}

`chunk(size)` still pulls the source once for every element, and groups what it pulls into batches. So it adds a little to each element, rather than saving anything there. It pays when the batch is what costs: one call to a warehouse or a database for ten orders, in place of ten calls. With `parEvalMap` after it, that is also one task, and one thread, for each batch rather than each element. A larger batch means fewer calls, more elements held at once, and a longer wait for the first result.

## Keeping a pipeline cheap {#keeping-a-pipeline-cheap}

- **Keep work that only computes** in `map` and `filter`, on the reading thread, where each adds a little to every element: far less than a task on a thread of its own.
- **Put work that waits in a task:** `mapTask` for one element at a time, and [`VStreamPar`](vstream_parallel.md) for several at once.
- **Batch a call that takes several elements** with `chunk`, then `parEvalMap`, as [Chunking operations](vstream_parallel.md#chunking-operations) shows.
- **Set the concurrency** to what the service accepts, as [Parallel Operations](vstream_parallel.md) explains.

## Measuring it yourself {#measuring-it-yourself}

`VStreamBenchmark`, in `hkj-benchmarks`, measures these costs with JMH:

```bash
./gradlew :hkj-benchmarks:jmh -Pincludes=".*VStreamBenchmark.*"
```

That run is quick, with the defaults the [Options](../benchmarks.md#options) table gives. For figures worth comparing, run more iterations in more forks, and add the GC profiler to see what each operation allocates:

```bash
./gradlew :hkj-benchmarks:jmh -Pincludes=".*VStreamBenchmark.*" \
  -Pjmh.warmupIterations=3 -Pjmh.iterations=5 -Pjmh.fork=2 -Pjmh.profilers=gc
```

The results go to `hkj-benchmarks/build/reports/jmh/`. Each score is operations per microsecond, so higher is faster, as [Reading the Output](../benchmarks.md#reading-the-output) explains. A filtered run like these overwrites `results.json`, and the next build's benchmark tests then fail on it, so delete the file before you build.

| Benchmarks | What they compare |
|---|---|
| `constructEmpty`, `constructSingle`, `constructFromList`, `constructRange`, and each `…Construction` | Building a stream or a pipeline, which runs nothing |
| `mapExecution`, `filterExecution`, `flatMapExecution`, `takeExecution`, `concatExecution`, `toListExecution`, `foldLeftExecution`, `countExecution` | Running one operation |
| `pipelineFilterMap`, `pipelineMapFilterTake`, `deepMapChainExecution`, `deepFlatMapChainExecution`, `realWorldTransformPipeline`, `realWorldFlatMapFilter` | Running pipelines |
| `baselineJavaStreamMap`, `baselineJavaStreamFilter`, `baselineJavaStreamPipeline`, `baselineJavaStreamFold` | The same work as a Java `Stream` |
| `mapTaskExecution`, `recoverThroughOperators` | A task for each element, and recovering through operators |
| `parEvalMapConcurrency2`, `parEvalMapConcurrency4`, `parEvalMapUnbounded`, `parEvalMapUnorderedConcurrency4` | Parallel tasks |
| `chunkExecution`, `mapChunkedExecution`, `realWorldChunkParallel` | Chunking |
| `existsEarlyMatch`, `findEarlyMatch`, `existsNoMatch` | Stopping early, against reading to the end |

Every benchmark works on data in memory, so it measures the stream's own cost. None waits on I/O, so none shows what a parallel operation gains for work that waits: the parallel benchmarks measure the cost of the threads. To see what parallel work gains for your pipeline, time it against the service it calls.

---

**Previous:** [Parallel Operations](vstream_parallel.md)
**Next:** [Resource-Safe Streaming](vstream_resources.md)

<!-- Source: monads/vstream_resources.md -->

<!-- description: Release what a VStream opens with bracket and onFinalize: once for each run, however the run ends, even when the consumer stops early. -->

# Resource-Safe Streaming: Release What a Stream Opens

_Open a connection when a run starts, and close it however the run ends._

~~~admonish info title="What You'll Learn"
- Open a resource for each run with `bracket`, and say when it is released, even when `take` stops early
- Predict what a run reports when reading fails, and when the release itself fails
- Nest brackets, and read one resource after another
- Attach cleanup to any stream with `onFinalize`
- Close the tail you hold when you pull by hand
~~~

~~~admonish example title="See Example Code"
**The code on this page is [VStreamResourcesBook.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/main/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/vstream/VStreamResourcesBook.java) and [VStreamLinesBook.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/main/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/vstream/VStreamLinesBook.java):** the page includes them, so the build compiles and runs them. [VStreamResourcesBookTest.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/vstream/VStreamResourcesBookTest.java) holds what the page says about each release.
~~~

---

## A stream that holds a connection {#a-stream-that-holds-a-connection}

A stream reads nothing until it runs, so a `try`-with-resources block around the code that builds it closes the connection before the first element is read. The connection has to be opened by the run, and closed when the run ends.

The code reuses the order feed from the [VStream](vstream.md#reading-a-feed-one-page-at-a-time) page. `orders(feed)` streams every order in a feed, and `PagedFeed` serves `ORDERS`, six orders of which four are paid, two to a page. `new PagedFeed(ORDERS, 2)` fails at page 2, and `shippingQuotes(orders, courier)` asks a courier to quote for each paid order. With `Order` from the [Optics](../optics/ch_intro.md) chapter, these are the imports:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import java.io.Closeable;
import java.io.IOException;
import java.math.BigDecimal;
import java.util.Collections;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.concurrent.CopyOnWriteArrayList;
import org.higherkindedj.example.book.optics.cast.Order;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.Unit;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.effect.Path;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.effect.VStreamPath;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.trymonad.Try;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStream;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vtask.VTask;
```

The feed is now read over a connection, which a service opens. `OrderFeed` and `Courier` are the VStream page's services:

```java
  // A service opens a connection to the order feed, or a session with a courier. Each is
  // closed once the stream is done with it, and opening or closing may throw an IOException
  interface FeedService {
    FeedConnection open() throws IOException;
  }

  interface FeedConnection extends OrderFeed, Closeable {}

  interface CourierService {
    CourierSession open() throws IOException;
  }

  interface CourierSession extends Courier, Closeable {}

```

## `bracket` opens and closes {#bracket-opens-and-closes}

`VStream.bracket(acquire, use, release)` opens a resource when a run starts, reads the stream `use` builds from it, and releases it however the run ends:

```java
  // Every order, read over a connection that is opened when a run starts and closed however
  // the run ends
  static VStream<Order> ordersOver(FeedService service) {
    return VStream.bracket(
        VTask.of(service::open), VStreamBook::orders, VStreamResourcesBook::closing);
  }

  // close() can throw an IOException, so it runs in VTask.of; VTask.exec takes a Runnable,
  // which cannot throw one
  static VTask<Unit> closing(Closeable resource) {
    return VTask.of(
        () -> {
          resource.close();
          return Unit.INSTANCE;
        });
  }

```

`LoggedFeedService(log)` serves `ORDERS` over each connection it opens, and writes each open, page and close to `log`, so the log shows what a run did:

```java
    List<String> log = new CopyOnWriteArrayList<>();
    VStream<String> paidCustomers =
        ordersOver(new LoggedFeedService(log)).filter(VStreamBook::paid).map(VStreamBook::customer);
    List<String> beforeRunning = List.copyOf(log);
    // []
    List<String> paid = paidCustomers.toList().run();
    // [Ada, Alan, Edsger, Ken]
    List<String> afterOneRun = List.copyOf(log);
    // [feed opened, page 1, page 2, page 3, page 4, feed closed]
```

Building the stream opened nothing. The run opened the connection, read every page up to the empty page 4, which ends the feed, and closed it. Each run opens a connection of its own, and closes it:

```java
    paidCustomers.toList().run();
    int opened = Collections.frequency(log, "feed opened");
    // 2
    int closed = Collections.frequency(log, "feed closed");
    // 2
```

Two threads can read one stream at the same time, each run with a connection of its own. A run closes only what it opened. So `take(0)` on one thread, which stops before it reads, leaves the other thread's connection open.

## Stopping early {#stopping-early}

A consumer that stops before the end still closes the connection:

```java
    List<String> earlyLog = new CopyOnWriteArrayList<>();
    List<String> firstTwo =
        ordersOver(new LoggedFeedService(earlyLog))
            .filter(VStreamBook::paid)
            .map(VStreamBook::customer)
            .take(2)
            .toList()
            .run();
    // [Ada, Alan]
    List<String> earlyRun = List.copyOf(earlyLog);
    // [feed opened, page 1, page 2, feed closed]
```

`take(2)` closes the rest of the stream once it has its two elements, so page 3 is never read. `takeWhile`, `zipWith`, `headOption`, `find`, `exists` and `forAll` close the rest in the same way when they stop.

## A file, a line at a time {#a-file-a-line-at-a-time}

A file is read the same way, with `java.nio.file.Files`, `java.nio.file.Path` and `java.util.Optional`. The reader opens when a run starts, and closes when the run reaches the end of the file, fails, or stops early:

```java
  // Each line of a file. The reader is opened when a run starts, and closed when the run
  // reaches the end of the file, fails, or stops early
  static VStream<String> lines(Path file) {
    return VStream.bracket(
        VTask.of(() -> Files.newBufferedReader(file)),
        reader ->
            VStream.unfold(
                reader,
                current ->
                    VTask.of(
                        () ->
                            Optional.ofNullable(current.readLine())
                                .map(line -> new VStream.Seed<>(line, current)))),
        VStreamResourcesBook::closing);
  }

```

```java
      // A file of three lines: Ada,PAID then Grace,NEW then Alan,PAID
      Files.write(ordersCsv, List.of("Ada,PAID", "Grace,NEW", "Alan,PAID"));
      List<String> firstTwo = lines(ordersCsv).take(2).toList().run();
      // [Ada,PAID, Grace,NEW]
```

`java.nio.file.Path` and the library's `Path` share a name, so code that uses both imports one and writes the other in full. The example keeps the file in a class of its own.

## When something fails {#when-something-fails}

A failure closes the connection too, and a close that fails is reported. `new LoggedFeedService(log, feed)` serves another feed, and `LoggedFeedService.failingToClose(log, feed)` gives connections whose close throws:

```java
    List<String> downLog = new CopyOnWriteArrayList<>();
    Try<List<Order>> feedDown =
        ordersOver(new LoggedFeedService(downLog, new PagedFeed(ORDERS, 2))).toList().runSafe();
    // Failure(java.io.IOException: the order feed is down)
    List<String> downRun = List.copyOf(downLog);
    // [feed opened, page 1, page 2, feed closed]

    Try<List<Order>> stuck =
        ordersOver(LoggedFeedService.failingToClose(downLog, new PagedFeed(ORDERS)))
            .toList()
            .runSafe();
    // Failure(java.io.IOException: the connection would not close)
```

| What fails | What happens |
|---|---|
| Reading, such as a page of the feed | The connection is closed, then the stream fails with the reading's own exception |
| `use`, while it builds the stream | The connection is closed, then the stream fails with `use`'s exception |
| The release, after a run that succeeded | The operation that ended the run, such as `toList`, fails with the release's exception |
| The release, after a run that failed | Its exception is suppressed onto the run's failure, in `getSuppressed()` |

`runSafe()` gives each failure as it was thrown. `run()` hands a checked one over inside a `VTaskExecutionException`, with the original as its cause, as [Running a task](vtask_monad.md#running-a-task) explains.

## Nested brackets, and one resource after another {#nested-brackets}

A bracket inside another holds its resource inside the outer one's, and releases it first. Here a courier session, whose quotes are 4.99 as `COURIER`'s are, is opened inside the feed connection:

```java
  // A courier session opened inside the feed connection, so it is closed first
  static VStream<BigDecimal> quotesOver(FeedService feeds, CourierService couriers) {
    return VStream.bracket(
        VTask.of(feeds::open),
        connection ->
            VStream.bracket(
                VTask.of(couriers::open),
                session -> shippingQuotes(orders(connection), session),
                VStreamResourcesBook::closing),
        VStreamResourcesBook::closing);
  }

```

```java
    List<String> nestedLog = new CopyOnWriteArrayList<>();
    List<BigDecimal> quotes =
        quotesOver(new LoggedFeedService(nestedLog), new LoggedCourierService(nestedLog))
            .toList()
            .run();
    // [4.99, 4.99, 4.99, 4.99]
    List<String> nestedRun = List.copyOf(nestedLog);
    // [feed opened, courier opened, page 1, page 2, page 3, page 4, courier closed, feed closed]
```

To read one resource after another, join the brackets outside each other, with `concat`. Each connection is closed before the next is opened:

```java
    // Two feeds, each read over its own connection, joined outside both brackets
    List<String> twoFeedsLog = new CopyOnWriteArrayList<>();
    VStream<Order> london =
        ordersOver(new LoggedFeedService(twoFeedsLog, new PagedFeed(ORDERS.subList(0, 2))));
    VStream<Order> edinburgh =
        ordersOver(new LoggedFeedService(twoFeedsLog, new PagedFeed(ORDERS.subList(2, 4))));

    long both = london.concat(edinburgh).count().run();
    // 4
    List<String> twoFeedsRun = List.copyOf(twoFeedsLog);
    // [feed opened, page 1, page 2, feed closed, feed opened, page 1, page 2, feed closed]
```

A `use` such as `connection -> orders(connection).concat(next)` instead keeps the first connection open until `next` has been read too.

## `onFinalize`: cleanup for any stream {#onfinalize-lightweight-cleanup}

`onFinalize(task)` runs a task when the stream's run ends, with nothing to open. It suits cleanup such as a log line or a metric:

```java
    List<String> reports = new CopyOnWriteArrayList<>();
    VStream<String> customers =
        orders(new PagedFeed(ORDERS))
            .map(VStreamBook::customer)
            .onFinalize(VTask.exec(() -> reports.add("customers read")))
            .onFinalize(VTask.exec(() -> reports.add("report sent")));

    List<String> firstCustomer = customers.take(1).toList().run();
    // [Ada]
    List<String> afterTake = List.copyOf(reports);
    // [customers read, report sent]
```

- **A finaliser runs once for each run**, when the run completes, fails, or is closed early by an operation that stops.
- **A stream the run never reaches is left as it is.** When a run stops before it reads `concat`'s second stream, `flatMap`'s stream for an element or the stream after `prepend`'s element, that stream's finaliser does not run. To run one regardless, attach it after the `concat` or `prepend`.
- **Several finalisers run in the order they were attached.**
- **After a recovered failure, the finaliser runs when the stream ends.** When a `recover` reads on after a failed `mapTask` task, the run goes on, and the finaliser waits for its end.
- **A failing finaliser is reported as a failing release is**, as [When something fails](#when-something-fails) shows. When several fail, the others still run, the first one's exception is thrown, and each later one is suppressed onto it.

`bracket` is built on `onFinalize`: its release is the finaliser of the stream that `use` gives.

## On a `VStreamPath` {#on-a-vstreampath}

[`VStreamPath`](../effect/path_vstream.md) has both: `Path.vstreamBracket` builds one, and `onFinalize` attaches a finaliser:

```java
    List<String> pathLog = new CopyOnWriteArrayList<>();
    FeedService service = new LoggedFeedService(pathLog);
    VStreamPath<Order> viaPath =
        Path.vstreamBracket(
                VTask.of(service::open), VStreamBook::orders, VStreamResourcesBook::closing)
            .onFinalize(VTask.exec(() -> pathLog.add("orders read")));

    long count = viaPath.count().unsafeRun();
    // 6
    List<String> pathRun = List.copyOf(pathLog);
    // [feed opened, page 1, page 2, page 3, page 4, feed closed, orders read]
```

The connection's release runs before the finaliser attached after it.

## Pulling by hand {#pulling-by-hand}

~~~admonish warning title="Close the tail you hold when you pull by hand"
A release runs when its run completes, fails or is closed. Pull steps by hand, as [What a pull gives](vstream.md#what-a-pull-gives) shows, and stop part-way, and the connection stays open until you close the tail you hold. Closing the stream you called `pull()` on closes no run's connection, since another thread's run may be reading it. The first pull of `ordersOver` opens the connection and gives a `Skip`, so even one pull leaves something to close. A pull that throws releases what it opened by itself.

`take` closes the rest only on the pull after its last element, so a hand puller who stops at that `Emit` still closes. A `Fail` step, for a failed `mapTask` task, holds the rest of the stream in its tail: close it, or read on from it, as you would an `Emit`'s. Running a pulled `VTask` twice opens two connections, one for each run, so close the tail each run gave you.
~~~

## Which thread releases {#which-thread-releases}

A release, or a finaliser, runs on the thread that makes the stream's last pull or closes it. That is the thread running the terminal operation, unless a parallel operation reads the stream on a thread of its own, as `parEvalMapUnordered` and `merge` do.

If that thread was interrupted before the release starts, the interrupt is held back while the release runs, and set again afterwards, so a release that blocks still finishes. An interrupt that arrives while the release runs, as a timeout's can, still reaches it.

## Methods {#methods}

| Method | What it does |
|---|---|
| `VStream.bracket(acquire, use, release)` | Opens a resource for each run, reads the stream `use` builds, and releases it however the run ends |
| `stream.onFinalize(task)` | Runs `task` once for each run, when the run ends |
| `stream.close()` | On the tail a run holds, ends that run: releases what it opened, and runs its finalisers. On a stream a run has pulled, ends no run. On a stream no run has pulled, runs the finalisers upstream of it |
| `Path.vstreamBracket(acquire, use, release)`, `path.onFinalize(task)` | The same, on a `VStreamPath` |

## When to use bracket and onFinalize {#when-to-use-them}

| You need | Use |
|---|---|
| A resource opened for each run of a stream, and closed after | `bracket` |
| Cleanup with nothing to open, such as a log line or a metric | `onFinalize` |
| Several resources read one after another | Brackets joined with `concat` |
| A resource for one task, not a stream | [Resource](vtask_resource.md) |

~~~admonish info title="Hands-On Learning"
Practise `bracket` and `onFinalize` in Part 1 of [TutorialVStreamAdvanced](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/tutorial/concurrency/TutorialVStreamAdvanced.java) (exercises 1 to 3).
~~~

# Advanced Features: Connecting VStream to Other APIs

_Bridge a stream to optics, `Flow.Publisher`, `List`, `Stream` and Paths, or run it without a `VTask`._

~~~admonish info title="What You'll Learn"
- Change a field of every element in a list or a stream with a `StreamTraversal`, reading a stream only as far as the consumer pulls
- Hand a stream to a `Flow.Subscriber`, and read a `Flow.Publisher` as a stream
- Convert between `VStream`, `List` and Java's `Stream`, and move a Path between them with a `Natural`
- Find a Path for a `Kind` with `Path.from`, and register a provider of your own
- Get a stream's answer directly, with no `VTask` to run, through `VStreamContext`
~~~

~~~admonish example title="See Example Code"
**The code on this page is [VStreamAdvancedBook.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/main/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/vstream/VStreamAdvancedBook.java)**: the page includes it, so the build compiles and runs it. [VStreamAdvancedBookTest.java](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/example/book/foundations/vstream/VStreamAdvancedBookTest.java) holds what the page says each method does.
~~~

---

Each section stands alone: [optics over a stream](#streamtraversal-lazy-optics), [a `Flow.Publisher`](#reactive-interop-flowpublisher-bridge), [`List` and `Stream`](#natural-transformations), [answers without a `VTask`](#vstreamcontext-layer-2-api), and, for code that builds Paths generically, [`Path.from`](#pathprovider-spi-registration).

The code on this page uses the order feed from the [VStream](vstream.md#reading-a-feed-one-page-at-a-time) page. `VStreamBook.PagedFeed` is a feed that records each page it is asked for, and `VStreamBook.orders(feed)` is the stream of its orders. `customer` and `paid` read an order. These are the page's imports:

<!-- verify -->
```java
import static org.higherkindedj.hkt.instances.Witnesses.list;
import static org.higherkindedj.hkt.instances.Witnesses.vstream;
import static org.higherkindedj.hkt.list.ListKindHelper.LIST;
import static org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStreamKindHelper.VSTREAM;

import java.math.BigDecimal;
import java.math.RoundingMode;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Optional;
import java.util.concurrent.Flow;
import org.higherkindedj.example.book.optics.cast.LineItem;
import org.higherkindedj.example.book.optics.cast.LineItemLenses;
import org.higherkindedj.example.book.optics.cast.Order;
import org.higherkindedj.example.book.optics.cast.OrderLenses;
import org.higherkindedj.example.book.optics.cast.OrderStatus;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.Natural;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.effect.GenericPath;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.effect.Path;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.effect.VStreamTransformations;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.effect.capability.Chainable;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.effect.context.VStreamContext;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.instances.Instances;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.list.ListKind;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStream;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStreamKind;
import org.higherkindedj.hkt.vstream.VStreamReactive;
import org.higherkindedj.optics.Traversal;
import org.higherkindedj.optics.extensions.StreamTraversal;
import org.higherkindedj.optics.util.Traversals;
```

## Optics over a stream {#streamtraversal-lazy-optics}

To change one field of every element a list or a stream gives, such as each order's status, use a `StreamTraversal`. Like a [`Traversal`](../optics/traversals.md) from the [Optics](../optics/ch_intro.md) chapter, it reaches every focus in a structure. A `Traversal` reads them all at once, through an `Applicative`. A `StreamTraversal` gives them as a `VStream`, so over a stream it reads only as far as the consumer pulls.

`forList()` focuses on each element of a list, and `andThen` with a `Lens` narrows each focus to one field, here an order line's price:

```java
  // A lamp, and four bulbs
  static final List<LineItem> LINES =
      List.of(
          new LineItem("LAMP", 1, new BigDecimal("40.00")),
          new LineItem("BULB", 4, new BigDecimal("2.50")));

  // Every price in an order's lines
  static final StreamTraversal<List<LineItem>, BigDecimal> PRICES =
      StreamTraversal.<LineItem>forList().andThen(LineItemLenses.price());

  static BigDecimal withTenPercent(BigDecimal price) {
    return price.multiply(new BigDecimal("1.10")).setScale(2, RoundingMode.HALF_UP);
  }

```

```java
    List<BigDecimal> prices = PRICES.stream(LINES).toList().run();
    // [40.00, 2.50]
    List<LineItem> raised = PRICES.modify(VStreamAdvancedBook::withTenPercent, LINES);
    BigDecimal lampPrice = raised.getFirst().price();
    // 44.00
```

`forVStream()` focuses on each element of a stream, so with `OrderLenses.status()` it reaches the status of every order the feed gives:

```java
  // The status of every order a stream gives
  static final StreamTraversal<VStream<Order>, OrderStatus> STATUSES =
      StreamTraversal.<Order>forVStream().andThen(OrderLenses.status());

  // A new order counts as paid, here
  static OrderStatus markPaid(OrderStatus status) {
    return status == OrderStatus.NEW ? OrderStatus.PAID : status;
  }

```

```java
    VStreamBook.PagedFeed feed = new VStreamBook.PagedFeed(ORDERS);
    VStream<Order> allPaid =
        STATUSES.modify(VStreamAdvancedBook::markPaid, VStreamBook.orders(feed));

    List<OrderStatus> firstTwo = allPaid.take(2).map(Order::status).toList().run();
    // [PAID, PAID]
    List<Integer> pagesFetched = feed.fetched();
    // [1]
```

Over a stream, `modify` gives a new stream, which changes each element as it is pulled. Two orders are taken, so only the feed's first page is fetched, and Grace's order, which was new, comes out paid. An endless stream works the same way. Written by hand, this is `orders.map(order -> OrderLenses.status().modify(...))`. A `StreamTraversal` is one value that serves a list or a stream, composes with lenses, and converts to a `Traversal`.

`modifyVTask` changes each focus with a task, and starts no thread of its own. Over a list, it returns one task that runs the element tasks in turn. Over a stream, that task gives the new stream at once, and each element's task runs as the element is pulled, on the thread that pulls it.

| | `Traversal` | `StreamTraversal` |
|---|---|---|
| Reads the focuses | All at once, through an `Applicative` | As a `VStream`; from `forVStream()`, as the consumer pulls |
| An endless stream | Never finishes reading it | From `forVStream()`, `stream`, `modify` and `modifyVTask` read it as pulled |
| Effects | Any `Applicative` | `VTask` |

### Converting to a Traversal {#conversion-to-standard-traversal}

`toTraversal()` gives a `Traversal` for code that takes one, such as `Traversals.getAll`:

```java
    Traversal<List<LineItem>, BigDecimal> asTraversal = PRICES.toTraversal();
    BigDecimal unitPrices =
        Traversals.getAll(asTraversal, LINES).stream().reduce(BigDecimal.ZERO, BigDecimal::add);
    // 42.50
```

`getAll` gives the two unit prices, which sum to 42.50. Over a stream, the `Traversal` reads the stream once, and the stream `Traversals.modify` gives can be read again. `StreamTraversal.fromTraversal(traversal)` goes the other way, and reads every focus as soon as `stream` is called.

## Exchanging elements with a `Flow.Publisher` {#reactive-interop-flowpublisher-bridge}

Java's `Flow.Publisher` sends elements to a subscriber as fast as the subscriber asks for them, which is how reactive libraries hand data on. A `VStream` is pulled instead. `VStreamReactive` joins the two.

### VStream to Publisher {#vstream-to-publisher}

`VStreamReactive.toPublisher(stream)` gives a publisher that pulls an element only when its subscriber has asked for one, plus one more to notice the end of the stream. Each subscriber reads the stream from the start. When a subscriber cancels, or the stream fails, the publisher closes what is left of the stream, so its finalisers run. A subscriber that cancels before it asks for anything closes nothing, since it has read nothing.

### Publisher to VStream {#publisher-to-vstream}

`VStreamReactive.fromPublisher(publisher, bufferSize)` reads a publisher as a stream. Here the stream of customers goes out through a publisher and is read back:

```java
    VStream<String> customers = VStream.fromList(ORDERS).map(VStreamBook::customer);

    Flow.Publisher<String> publisher = VStreamReactive.toPublisher(customers);
    VStream<String> readBack = VStreamReactive.fromPublisher(publisher, 16);

    List<String> firstThree = readBack.take(3).toList().run();
    // [Ada, Grace, Alan]
```

- **Each run subscribes at its first pull**, so building the stream subscribes nothing, and a stream read twice subscribes twice.
- **A hot publisher**, such as a `SubmissionPublisher`, sends a run only what it publishes after the run subscribes. Publish once `getNumberOfSubscribers()` shows the run has subscribed.
- **Incoming elements are held in a buffer of `bufferSize`**, 256 if you leave it out. The stream first asks for that many, then for one more as it takes each element.
- **A run that stops early cancels its subscription**, so `take(3)` stops the publisher once it has its three elements.

The Flow rules forbid a null element, and so does a `VStream`. A publisher that sends one has its subscription cancelled, and the stream fails with a `NullPointerException`: `VStream.fromPublisher element cannot be null`.

On a `VStreamPath`, `Path.vstreamFromPublisher(publisher, bufferSize)` reads a publisher, and `path.toPublisher()` hands the stream to one.

## Converting between VStream, List and Stream {#natural-transformations}

For one stream, `toList().run()` gives a `List`. `VStreamTransformations` is for code written over `Kind`, which takes a conversion as a value. It gives a [`Natural`](../functional/natural_transformation.md) for each direction between `VStream` and `List`, and between `VStream` and Java's `Stream`. A `Natural` converts one container to another whatever its elements, and `GenericPath.mapK` takes one, to move a path onto another container. The [Foundations](../hkts/foundations_intro.md) introduction's "Which level am I working at?" tip says when code works over `Kind`.

```java
    Natural<ListKind.Witness, VStreamKind.Witness> listToVStream =
        VStreamTransformations.listToVStream();
    Natural<VStreamKind.Witness, ListKind.Witness> vstreamToList =
        VStreamTransformations.vstreamToList();

    GenericPath<ListKind.Witness, Order> asList =
        GenericPath.of(LIST.widen(ORDERS), Instances.monad(list()));
    GenericPath<VStreamKind.Witness, Order> asStream =
        asList.mapK(listToVStream, Instances.monad(vstream()));

    long paid = VSTREAM.narrow(asStream.runKind()).filter(VStreamBook::paid).count().run();
    // 4
    List<Order> roundTrip =
        LIST.narrow(listToVStream.andThen(vstreamToList).apply(LIST.widen(ORDERS)));
    int orders = roundTrip.size();
    // 6
```

`listToVStream` and `streamToVStream` read nothing until the stream is pulled. `vstreamToList` and `vstreamToStream` read the whole stream when they are applied, on the calling thread, so take a prefix of an endless stream first. A Java `Stream` can be read only once, so each run of a `VStream` made from one carries on from where the last run stopped, and after a full run it gives nothing.

## Finding a Path, and registering a provider {#pathprovider-spi-registration}

`Path.from(kind, witness)` asks the `PathProvider` registered for `witness` for a Path that holds `kind`. `VStreamPathProvider` answers for a `VStream`:

```java
    Optional<Chainable<?, Order>> found =
        Path.from(VSTREAM.widen(VStream.fromList(ORDERS)), VStreamKind.Witness.class);
    boolean hasPath = found.isPresent();
    // true
```

The registry cannot know which Path a provider builds, so the result's [family](../effect/capabilities.md#the-family-parameter) is open, the `?` in `Chainable<?, Order>`. It can be mapped, but `via` and `zipWith` accept no step. To chain, build the Path directly with `Path.vstream(...)`.

The provider is registered in `META-INF/services/org.higherkindedj.hkt.effect.spi.PathProvider` and in the `provides` clause of `hkj-core`'s `module-info`, so `Path.from()` finds it whether `hkj-core` is on the classpath or the module path. To register a `PathProvider` of your own, list it in the same services file, which `ServiceLoader` reads when the jar is on the classpath. In a module with a `module-info`, also declare `provides org.higherkindedj.hkt.effect.spi.PathProvider with ...;`, which is all it reads on the module path.

A provider whose effect can fail overrides `monadError()` to return its `MonadError` in an `Optional`. The default is empty, as for `VStream`, and `supportsRecovery()` returns whether one is present. `PathRegistry`, which holds the providers, gives the one it removed from `unregister` in an `Optional` too.

## Answers without a `VTask`: `VStreamContext` {#vstreamcontext-layer-2-api}

A `VStream`'s terminal operations, such as `toList`, give a `VTask` to run. A `VStreamContext` runs the stream itself, on the calling thread, and returns the answer. It works like the [Effect Contexts](../effect/effect_contexts.md#the-three-layer-architecture), such as `VTaskContext`, and has many of a `VStream`'s operations: `map`, `via`, `filter`, `take`, `parEvalMap` and `chunk` among them:

```java
    List<String> firstPaid =
        VStreamContext.fromList(ORDERS)
            .filter(VStreamBook::paid)
            .map(VStreamBook::customer)
            .take(3)
            .toList();
    // [Ada, Alan, Edsger]
    long shipped =
        VStreamContext.fromList(ORDERS)
            .filter(order -> order.status() == OrderStatus.SHIPPED)
            .count();
    // 1
```

A stream that fails throws from the terminal operation, with a checked exception wrapped in a `VTaskExecutionException`. `toVStream()` and `toPath()` give the stream, and a `VStreamPath` over it, when you need them.

## Methods {#methods}

| Method | What it does |
|---|---|
| `StreamTraversal.forList()`, `forVStream()` | Focuses on each element of a list, or of a stream |
| `streamTraversal.andThen(lens)`, `andThen(other)` | Narrows each focus to a field, or to another `StreamTraversal`'s focuses |
| `streamTraversal.stream(source)` | Gives the focuses as a `VStream` |
| `streamTraversal.modify(f, source)` | Changes each focus with `f` |
| `streamTraversal.modifyVTask(f, source)` | Changes each focus with a task |
| `streamTraversal.toTraversal()`, `StreamTraversal.fromTraversal(traversal)` | Converts to and from a `Traversal` |
| `VStreamReactive.toPublisher(stream)` | Hands the stream to each subscriber that asks |
| `VStreamReactive.fromPublisher(publisher, bufferSize)`, `fromPublisher(publisher)` | Reads a publisher as a stream, holding up to `bufferSize` elements, or 256 |
| `Path.vstreamFromPublisher(publisher, bufferSize)`, `vstreamPath.toPublisher()` | Do the same on a `VStreamPath` |
| `VStreamTransformations.listToVStream()`, `vstreamToList()`, `streamToVStream()`, `vstreamToStream()` | Give the `Natural`s between `VStream`, `List` and `Stream` |
| `genericPath.mapK(natural, monad)` | Moves a path onto another container |
| `Path.from(kind, witness)` | Finds a Path for `kind` through the provider registered for `witness` |
| `VStreamContext.fromList(list)`, `of(stream)`, `range(from, to)` | Start a context |
| `context.map(f)`, `via(f)`, `filter(p)`, `take(n)` | Build the next context |
| `context.toList()`, `count()`, `fold(identity, op)`, `find(p)` | Run the stream, and give the answer |
| `context.toVStream()`, `toPath()` | Give the stream, or a `VStreamPath` over it |

## When to use each bridge {#when-to-use-them}

| You need | Use |
|---|---|
| One field of every element changed, in a list or a stream | `StreamTraversal`, with a `Lens` |
| Elements from a reactive library, or handed to one | `VStreamReactive` |
| A `VStream`'s elements as a `List` or a `Stream`, or the other way | `toList()`, or `VStreamTransformations` for code over `Kind` |
| A stream's answer with no `VTask` to run | `VStreamContext` |
| A Path for a `Kind` your code cannot name | `Path.from`, or `Path.vstream` to chain |
| Every focus of a structure in memory, read at once | A [`Traversal`](../optics/traversals.md) |

~~~admonish info title="Hands-On Learning"
Practise `StreamTraversal`, the publisher bridge, the transformations and `VStreamContext` in Parts 2 to 5 of [TutorialVStreamAdvanced](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/tutorial/concurrency/TutorialVStreamAdvanced.java) (exercises 4 to 12), and optics over a `VStream` in [Tutorial 17: VStream Optics](https://github.com/higher-kinded-j/higher-kinded-j/blob/main/hkj-examples/src/test/java/org/higherkindedj/tutorial/optics/Tutorial17_VStreamOptics.java) (8 exercises).
~~~

**Next:** [Reader](reader_monad.md)
